Log motivic Gysin isomorphisms
Doosung Park

Abstract
. In this article, we construct the Gysin isomorphisms in the axiomatic motivic setting for fs log schemes. We formulate the purity transformations for log smooth morphisms. We show that the purity transformations are isomorphisms for certain specific non-strict morphisms of fs log schemes.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14F42; Secondary 14A21

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Gysin isomorphisms in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory of schemes

Morel and Voevodsky
[
13
]
introduced
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory of schemes, which aims to study algebraic geometry by means of algebraic topology.
Many techniques in algebraic topology have been adapted to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory.
For example,
Morel and Voevodsky constructed
[
13
, Theorem 2.23 in §3]
the
Gysin isomorphisms
in the motivic setting as follows, which is analogous to the Gysin isomorphisms in algebraic topology:

Theorem 1.1.1
.

Let
S
be a noetherian scheme of finite dimension.
Then for every closed immersion
Z\to X
of smooth schemes over
S
,
there exists a natural isomorphism

X/(X-Z)\simeq\mathrm{N}_{Z}X/(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X-Z)

in the motivic pointed homotopy category
\mathcal{H}_{*}(S)
,
where
\mathrm{N}_{Z}X
is the normal bundle of
Z
in
X
, and
\mathrm{N}_{Z}X-Z
is the complement of the zero section.

The
Thom space
\mathrm{Th}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)
is defined to be the right hand side
\mathrm{N}_{Z}X/(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X-Z)
.
The Gysin isomorphism provides a fundamental relation among the motives of
X
,
X-Z
, and
\mathrm{Th}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X)
.

1.2.
Gysin isomorphisms in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory of fs log schemes

In
[
17
]
,
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory of fs log schemes was initiated.
The motivic stable homotopy category
\mathcal{SH}(S)
of Morel-Voevodsky was extended to the case when
S
is a noetherian fs log scheme of finite dimension.
Let us review the construction of
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
We begin with the
\infty
-category of dividing Nisnevich sheaves of spectra on the category
\mathbf{lSm}/S
of log smooth fs log schemes over
S
:

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}).

The dividing Nisnevich topology in
[
5
, Definition 3.1.4]
is a log variant of the Nisnevich topology.
We localize this
\infty
-category by the classes of the projections
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to X
and open immersions
X-\partial_{S}X\to X
for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
, where
\partial_{S}X
is the vertical boundary
[
17
, Definition 2.3.5]
.
After taking
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-stabilization, we obtain
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.

This article is a continuation of
[
17
]
.
Our first aim is to provide the log version of the Gysin isomorphisms.
We may ask the following question, where
B
is a fixed base scheme throughout this article:

•

For every noetherian fs log scheme of finite dimension
S
over
B
and closed immersion
Z\to X
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
, is there a natural isomorphism

\Sigma^{\infty}(X/X-Z)\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X/\mathrm{N}_{Z}X-Z)

in
\mathcal{SH}(S)
?

The first problem we encounter is that this is not formulated well since the reasonable open complement
X-Z
does not exist in the category of fs log schemes if
Z\to X
is not strict.

To resolve this, one may consider a suitable log blow-up
X^{\prime}\to X
such that the projection
Z^{\prime}:=Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
is a strict closed immersion.
Since our motivic setting inverts log blow-ups, we may instead construct an isomorphism

\Sigma^{\infty}(X^{\prime}/X^{\prime}-Z^{\prime})\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathrm{N}_{Z^{\prime}}X^{\prime}/\mathrm{N}_{Z^{\prime}}X^{\prime}-Z^{\prime})

in
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
However, there is a technical limitation of this approach: We do not know that such a log blow-up
X^{\prime}\to X
exists globally even though it exists locally as a consequence of
[
15
, Theorem III.2.6.7]
.
Therefore, one has to first consider a Zariski cover of
X
and then a log blow-up.
Then checking functoriality becomes more and more sophisticated.
This method was taken in
[
16
]
.

Our strategy for this problem is to use divided log spaces over the base scheme
B
developed in
[
18
]
, which we review in §
2.1
.
A
(noetherian) divided log space over
B
is a dividing Zariski sheaf of sets on the category
\mathbf{lSch}/B
of noetherian fs log schemes of finite dimensions over
B
satisfying certain axioms that are inspired by the axioms of algebraic spaces.
We refer to
[
5
, Definition 3.1.4]
for the dividing Zariski topology.
The Yoneda functor

h\colon\mathbf{lSch}/B\to\mathbf{Shv}_{dZar}(\mathbf{lSch}/B)

makes every object of
\mathbf{lSch}/B
into a divided log space.
There are notions of open immersions, closed immersions, and log smooth morphisms of divided log spaces.
We can also define the dividing Zariski and dividing Nisnevich topologies on the category of divided log spaces
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.

An advantage of divided log spaces is that a reasonable definition of the open complement of a closed immersion
a\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
exists.
Furthermore, if
a
is a closed immersion of log smooth divided log spaces over some
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, then reasonable definitions of the blow-up
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}
and the normal bundle
\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}
also exist.

To use divided log spaces in motivic homotopy theory, we need to extend
\mathcal{SH}
from fs log schemes to divided log spaces.
For flexibility of our theory, we instead fix a dividing Nisnevich sheaf of symmetric monoidal presentable
\infty
-categories

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

throughout this article satisfying the following conditions:

•

For every morphism
f
in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
,
f_{*}
denotes a right adjoint of
f^{*}:=\mathscr{T}(f)
.

•

For every log smooth morphism
f\colon X\to S
in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
,
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
.
We set
M_{S}(X):=f_{\sharp}\mathbf{1}\in\mathscr{T}(S)
, where
\mathbf{1}
denotes the monoidal unit in
\mathscr{T}(X)
.

•

(Log smooth base change)
For every cartesian square in
\mathbf{lSch}/B

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

such that
f
is log smooth, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}g^{*}f_{\sharp}

is an isomorphism.

•

(Log smooth projection formula)
For every log smooth morphism
f\colon X\to S
in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, the Beck-Chevalley transformation

f_{\sharp}((-)\otimes f^{*}(-))\xrightarrow{Ex}f_{\sharp}(-)\otimes(-)

is an isomorphism.

•

(Localization property) For every strict closed immersion
i\colon Z\to S
in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
with its open complement
j\colon U\to S
,
i_{*}
is fully faithful, and the pair of functors
(i^{*},j^{*})
is conservative.

•

(
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance)
Let
X\to S
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
p\colon X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to X
be the projection.
Then the morphism

M_{S}(p)\colon M_{S}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\to M_{S}(X)

is an isomorphism.

•

(
ver
-invariance)
Let
f\colon X\to S
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
j\colon X-\partial_{S}X\to X
be the obvious open immersion.
Then the morphism

M_{S}(j)\colon M_{S}(X-\partial_{S}X)\to M_{S}(X)

is an isomorphism.

•

(
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stability) Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
Then
\mathscr{T}(S)
is stable.
Furthermore,

\mathbf{1}(1):=\mathrm{cofib}(M_{S}(S)\xrightarrow{M_{S}(i_{0})}M_{S}(S\times\mathbb{P}^{1}))[-2]

admits a
\otimes
-inverse
\mathbf{1}(-1)
, where
i_{0}\colon S\to S\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the
0
-section.
We set
\mathbf{1}(d):=\mathbf{1}(1)^{\otimes d}
for every integer
d
.

•

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
The family
\{M_{S}(X)(d)[n]:X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S,\,d,n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
generates
\mathscr{T}(S)
.

We note that
\mathcal{SH}
satisfies all the above conditions for
\mathscr{T}
, see Example
2.0.1
.

We show in Proposition
2.2.1
that there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})),

where
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
denotes the category of divided log spaces over
B
.
From this, we obtain a naturally defined dividing Nisnevich sheaf

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})).

In particular, we obtain
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
for all
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
We show in §
2.2
that
\mathscr{T}
for divided log spaces satisfies similar conditions.
For this, we need the technique of partial adjoints developed by Liu-Zheng in
[
9
]
.

If
p\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
is a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, we set
M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}):=p_{\sharp}\mathbf{1}
, where
\mathbf{1}
denotes the monoidal unit in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})
.
If
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{X}
is an open immersion of log smooth divided log spaces over
\mathcal{S}
, we set
M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{U}):=\mathrm{cofib}(M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{U})\to M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}))
.
Using divided log spaces, our first main result can be formulated as follows.

Theorem 1.2.1

(Theorem
3.4.1
)
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
be a closed immersion of log smooth divided log spaces over
\mathcal{S}
, where
\mathcal{S}
is a divided log space.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})\simeq M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}/\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})

in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
.

1.3.
Thom transformations

As an application of the Gysin isomorphisms, we deduce the following result.

Theorem 1.3.1

(Theorem
4.2.5
)
.

Let
p\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, and let
a\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{X}
be its section. Assume that
a
is a closed immersion.
Then the natural transformation
\mathrm{Th}(p,a):=p_{\sharp}a_{*}
, which we call the
Thom transformation
, is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

We are mainly interested in the case when
p\colon X\to S
is a separated log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
and
a\colon S\to X
is its section.
Even for this case, we need Theorem
1.2.1
not just for fs log schemes but for divided log spaces since the open complement of
a
does not exist in the category of fs log schemes if
a
is not strict.

1.4.
Purity for a compactification of
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}

Suppose that
f\colon X\to S
is a proper morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
p_{1}
(resp.
p_{2}
) is the first (resp. second) projection, and
a
is the diagonal morphism.
We say that
f
is
\mathscr{T}
-pure
(or
pure
for brevity) if the composition

f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{\sharp}p_{1*}a_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}f_{*}p_{2\sharp}a_{*}=f_{*}\mathrm{Th}(p_{2},a)

is an isomorphism, see Example
A.1.4
for the notation
Ex
.
As observed in
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.2]
, the purity theorem of Voevodsky-Röndigs-Ayoub
[
3
, Theorem 1.7.17]
(see also
[
6
, Theorems 2.4.26, 2.4.28]
) implies the following result for strict smooth morphisms:

Theorem 1.4.1
.

Any strict separated smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
is pure.

The core part of the purity theorem is the case of the projection
S\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to S
.
The general case is a consequence of this and the localization property.
The purity theorem for schemes is not enough to establish the six-functor formalism for fs log schemes since there are lots of non-strict log smooth morphisms of fs log schemes.
Our last main result is to show that some specific non-strict proper morphisms of fs log schemes are pure as follows.
We refer to
[
15
, Definition III.1.2.3]
for the notation
\Spec(P\to A)
.

Theorem 1.4.2
.

Let
S\to B\times\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
be a morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
W
be the gluing of

\displaystyle V_{1}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}x\oplus\mathbb{N}y\to\mathbb{Z}[x,y]),

\displaystyle V_{2}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}(xy)\to\mathbb{Z}[xy,y^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{3}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}(xy)\to\mathbb{Z}[xy,x^{-1}])

along

\displaystyle V_{12}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}x\to\mathbb{Z}[x,y,y^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{13}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}y\to\mathbb{Z}[x,y,x^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{23}:=\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[x,x^{-1},y,y^{-1}]).

Consider the proper morphism
W\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}t\to\mathbb{Z}[t])
obtained by the formula
t\mapsto xy
.
Then the projection
f\colon W\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}S\to S
is pure.

This extra abundance of purity is a building block of the motivic six-functor formalism for fs log schemes in the forthcoming work
[
19
]
.

Notation and conventions

Our standard references for log geometry and
\infty
-categories are Ogus’s book
[
15
]
and Lurie’s books
[
10
]
and
[
11
]
.
We employ the following notation throughout this article:

B

base noetherian scheme of finite dimension

\mathbf{Sch}

category of noetherian schemes of finite dimensions

\mathbf{lSch}

category of noetherian fs log schemes of finite dimensions

\mathbf{lSpc}/B

category of divided log spaces over
B

\mathbf{lSm}

class of log smooth morphisms in
\mathbf{lSch}/B

\mathbf{lSmSpc}

class of log smooth morphisms in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B

\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})

category of presheaves of sets on
\mathcal{C}

\mathbf{Shv}_{t}(\mathcal{C})

category of
t
-sheaves of sets on
\mathcal{C}

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{V})

\infty
-category of presheaves with values in an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{V}
on
\mathcal{C}

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{t}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{V})

\infty
-category of
t
-sheaves with values in an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{V}
on
\mathcal{C}

\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}

\infty
-category of
\infty
-categories

{\rm id}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}f^{*}

unit of an adjunction
(f^{*},f_{*})

f^{*}f_{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}{\rm id}

counit of an adjunction
(f^{*},f_{*})

2.
Stable homotopy categories for divided log spaces

Recall from
[
5
, Definition 3.1.4]
that a cartesian square

(2.0.1)

{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -16.02 -24.46 L 19.35 -24.46}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{14.18335pt}{-17.67757pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.67343pt}{-13.96371pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
is a

(1)

Zariski distinguished square
if
Q\simeq\underline{Q}\times_{\underline{S}}S
and
\underline{Q}
is a Zariski distinguished square in
\mathbf{Sch}/B
,

(2)

strict Nisnevich distinguished square
if
Q\simeq\underline{Q}\times_{\underline{S}}S
and
\underline{Q}
is a Nisnevich distinguished square in
\mathbf{Sch}/B
,

(3)

dividing distinguished square
if
S^{\prime}=X^{\prime}=\emptyset
and
f
is a dividing cover, i.e., a surjective proper log étale monomorphism.

(4)

dividing Zariski distinguished square
if
Q
is either a Zariski or dividing distinguished square,

(5)

dividing Nisnevich distinguished square
if
Q
is either a strict Nisnevich or dividing distinguished square.

The
dividing Zariski
(resp.
dividing Nisnevich
)
cd-structure
is the collection of dividing Nisnevich distinguished square,
and its associated topology is the
dividing Zariski
(resp.
dividing Nisnevich
)
topology
.

Recall that we fix a sheaf

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

satisfying the conditions in the introduction.
The sheaf condition means that for every family
\{X_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
producing a dividing covering sieve,
we have an equivalence

(2.0.2)

\mathscr{T}(X)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\lim\big(\prod_{i\in I}\mathscr{T}(X_{i})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\prod_{i,j\in I}\mathscr{T}(X_{i}\times_{X}X_{j})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\cdots\big),

where the limit is taken in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
.
Here,
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
denotes the subcategory of
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
spanned by presentable
\infty
-categories and colimit preserving functors, and
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
denotes the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
.
By
[
11
, Example 3.1.3.4, Lemma 3.2.2.6]
, the forgetful functor
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
is conservative and preserves limits.
By
[
10
, Proposition 5.5.3.13]
, the inclusion functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
preserves limits.
Hence the sheaf condition on
\mathscr{T}
is equivalent to saying that we have the equivalence (
2.0.2
) when we forget the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathscr{T}
and the limit is taken in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
.

By Theorem
A.4.2
,
the sheaf condition on
\mathscr{T}
is equivalent to saying that the square of
\infty
-categories

(2.0.3)

\mathscr{T}(Q)=\hbox to112.17pt{\vbox to55.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 56.08328pt\lower-26.3373pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

is cartesian for every strict Nisnevich distinguished square
Q
of the form (
2.0.1
) and the morphism

f^{*}\colon\mathscr{T}(S)\to\mathscr{T}(X)

is an equivalence for every dividing cover
f\colon X\to S
in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.

Example 2.0.1
.

There are two fundamental examples of stable
\infty
-categories of motives over fs log schemes in
[
17
, Definition 2.5.5]
formulated as

\displaystyle\mathcal{SH}(S):=\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\mathbb{A}^{1}\cup ver)^{-1}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})),

\displaystyle\mathcal{DA}(S,\Lambda):=\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\mathbb{A}^{1}\cup ver)^{-1}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Mod}_{\Lambda}))),

where
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
denotes the
\infty
-category of spectra,
\Lambda
is a ring, and
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{Mod}_{\Lambda})
denotes the
\infty
-category of chain complexes of
\Lambda
-modules.
The class
ver_{S}
consists of open immersion
U\to V
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
such that the induced morphism
U-\partial_{S}U\to V-\partial_{S}V
is an isomorphism, where
\partial_{S}U
and
\partial_{S}V
are the vertical boundaries
[
17
, Definition 2.3.5]
.

Let us show that
\mathcal{SH}
and
\mathcal{DA}(-,\Lambda)
satisfy the above conditions for
\mathscr{T}
.
We only focus on the case of
\mathcal{SH}
since the proofs are similar.
For
V\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
, let
\Sigma^{\infty}V_{+}
denote the object of
\mathcal{SH}(S)
represented by
V
.

For every dividing Nisnevich distinguished square
Q
of the form (
2.0.1
) and
V\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
,
the induced square
\Sigma^{\infty}(Q\times V)_{+}
is cocartesian in
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
Use this to show that
\mathcal{SH}(Q)
is cartesian, i.e.,
\mathcal{SH}
is a dividing Nisnevich sheaf.

The existence of
f_{\sharp}
for
f\in\mathbf{lSm}
, log smooth base change, and log smooth projection formula are encoded in the fact that
\mathcal{SH}
is a
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-category, see
[
17
, §2.5]
.

Due to
[
17
, Theorem 3.3.9]
,
\mathcal{SH}
satisfies the localization property.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{SH}
satisfies the remaining conditions for
\mathscr{T}
as a direct consequence of the construction.

2.1.
Review of divided log spaces

Let us briefly review the theory of divided log spaces developed in
[
18
]
.

For a fan
\Sigma
, let
\underline{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}
be the toric variety associated with
\Sigma
, and let
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
be the fs log scheme whose underlying scheme is
\underline{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}
and whose log structure is the compactifying log structure associated with the open immersion from the dense torus of
\underline{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}
.

For
X\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
,
a
fan chart of
X
is a strict morphism
X\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
, where
\Sigma
is a fan.
Let
\mathbf{lFan}/B
be the full subcategory of
\mathbf{lSch}/B
consisting of fs log schemes of the form
\amalg_{i\in I}X_{i}
with finite
I
such that each
X_{i}
admits a fan chart.

For all
X\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
,
let
h_{X}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{dZar}(\mathbf{lSch}/B)
be the sheaf represented by
X
.
As observed in
[
18
, §7]
, there is an equivalence of categories

\mathbf{Shv}_{dZar}(\mathbf{lFan}/B)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{dZar}(\mathbf{lSch}/B).

Definition 2.1.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is a class of morphisms in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
closed under pullbacks.
A morphism of sheaves
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
in
\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lFan}/B)
is a
representable
\mathcal{P}
-morphism
if for every morphism
h_{X}\to\mathcal{F}
with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
, there exists a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{f}}

such that
f
is a
\mathcal{P}
-morphism in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
.

Definition 2.1.2
.

A
divided log space over
B
is a sheaf
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{dZar}(\mathbf{lFan}/B)
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

The diagonal morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
is a representable closed immersion.

(ii)

There exists a representable Zariski cover
h_{X}\to\mathcal{X}
with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.

A
morphism of divided log spaces over
B
is a morphism of sheaves.
The category of divided log spaces over
B
is denoted by
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.

For all
X\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
,
[
18
, Proposition 4.9]
shows
h_{X}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.

By
[
18
, Proposition 4.6]
, fiber product exists in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, and the inclusion functor
\mathbf{lSpc}/B\to\mathbf{Shv}_{dZar}(\mathbf{lFan}/B)
preserves fiber products.
It follows that the functor
h\colon\mathbf{lSch}/B\to\mathbf{lSpc}/B
preserves fiber products.

Proposition 2.1.3
.

Let
h_{U},h_{V}\to\mathcal{X}
be morphisms in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
with
U,V\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Then
h_{U}\times_{\mathcal{X}}h_{V}\simeq h_{W}
for some
W\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.

Proof.

We refer to
[
18
, Proposition 4.3]
.
∎

Definition 2.1.4
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is one of the following classes of morphisms in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
: log smooth morphisms, log étale morphisms, open immersions, closed immersions, Zariski covers, strict Nisnevich covers, strict étale covers, and Kummer étale covers.
A morphism
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
is a
\mathcal{P}
-morphism
if there exists a representable Zariski cover
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{Y}
such that the composition
\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{X}
is a representable
\mathcal{P}
-morphism.
We note that the class of
\mathcal{P}
-morphisms (resp. representable
\mathcal{P}
-morphisms) are closed under pullbacks and compositions by
[
18
, Proposition 6.5]
(resp.
[
18
, Example 3.9, Proposition 3.13]
).

Let
\mathbf{lSmSpc}
denote the class of log smooth morphisms in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.

Proposition 2.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be one of the above classes of morphisms in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Then a morphism
f\colon h_{Y}\to h_{X}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
with
X,Y\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
is a
\mathcal{P}
-morphism if and only if
f
is a representable
\mathcal{P}
-morphism.

Proof.

Consequence of
[
18
, Lemma 3.4, Proposition 6.3]
.
∎

Proposition 2.1.6
.

A morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
is an open immersion (resp. closed immersion) if and only if it is a representable open immersion (resp. representable closed immersion).

Proof.

We refer to
[
18
, Proposition 6.7]
.
∎

Definition 2.1.7
.

The
dividing Zariski
(resp.
dividing Nisnevich
)
topology on
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
is the topology generated by the Zariski (resp. strict Nisnevich) covers in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
By
[
18
, Proposition 7.2]
, it is the topology generated by the representable Zariski (resp. representable strict Nisnevich) covers.

Definition 2.1.8
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
be a closed immersion in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
The
open complement
\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z}
of
\mathcal{Z}
in
\mathcal{X}
is defined to be a final object of the full subcategory of
\mathbf{lSpc}/\mathcal{X}
consisting of
\mathcal{Y}
such that
\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{Z}=\emptyset
.
By
[
18
, Theorem 8.6]
,
\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z}
exists, and the canonical morphism
\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
is an open immersion.

Definition 2.1.9
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
be a closed immersion in
\mathbf{lSmSpc}/\mathcal{S}
, where
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
The
blow-up
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}
of
\mathcal{X}
along
\mathcal{Z}
is defined to be a final object of the full subcategory of
\mathbf{lSpc}/\mathcal{X}
consisting of
\mathcal{Y}
such that
\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{Y}
is an effective log Cartier divisor on
\mathcal{Y}
in the sense of
[
18
, Definition 9.1]
.
By
[
18
, Theorem 9.12]
,
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}
exists, and the canonical morphism
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
is log smooth.
We set

\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}:=\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathcal{Z}\times\{0\}}(\mathcal{X}\times\square)-\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathcal{Z}\times\{0\}}(\mathcal{X}\times\{0\})\text{ and }\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}:=\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}\times_{\square}\{0\}.

Here,
\square
denotes the fs log scheme whose underlying scheme is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and whose log structure is the compactifying log structure associated with the open immersion
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

2.2.
Extension of motives to divided log spaces

Liu-Zheng
[
9
]
extended the six-functor formalism from schemes to algebraic spaces (and more) using
\infty
-categories.
Likewise, we explain how to extend
\mathscr{T}
from
\mathbf{lSch}/B
to
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
in this subsection.
We also explore several fundamental properties of the extension of
\mathscr{T}
.

Proposition 2.2.1
.

For every presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{V}
,
there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathcal{V})\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathcal{V}).

Proof.

By
[
18
, Proposition 7.3]
,
there is an equivalence of categories.

\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B)\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B).

For every
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
there exists a Zariski cover
h_{X}\to\mathcal{X}
with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
By Proposition
2.1.3
,
the fiber product of finite copies of
h_{X}
over
\mathcal{X}
is isomorphic to
h_{V}
for some
V\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Since the functor
\mathbf{lSch}/B\to\mathbf{lSpc}/B
is continuous and cocontinuous,
Theorem
A.3.6
finishes the proof.
∎

In particular,
we obtain

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})).

If
f
is a morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, then we set
f^{*}:=\mathscr{T}(f)
.
The sheaf condition means that for every family
\{\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{X}\}_{i\in I}
producing a dividing Nisnevich covering sieve in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
we have an equivalence

(2.2.1)

\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\lim\big(\prod_{i\in I}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\prod_{i,j\in I}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{j})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\cdots\big),

where the limit is taken in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
.
If we forget the symmetric monoidal structures,
then the limit can be taken in either
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
or
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
since the forgetful functor
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
and the inclusion functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
preserve limits by
[
11
, Example 3.1.3.4]
and
[
10
, Proposition 5.5.3.13]
.

Suppose that
f\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
is a morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
and
\mathcal{X}_{0}\to\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\to\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{0}
are Zariski covers.
Let
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{Y}
be Čech nerves of
\mathcal{X}_{0}\to\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\to\mathcal{Y}
,
Then take limits along the rows in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}}

to obtain a functor

\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i}^{*}\colon\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})\to\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}).

Observe that there is a commutative square with horizontal equivalences

(2.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{i}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proposition 2.2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{0}\to\mathcal{X}
be a Zariski cover in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
and let
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{X}
be its Čech nerve.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

{\rm id}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i\in\Delta^{op}}f_{i_{\sharp}}f_{i}^{*},

where
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{X}
is the induced morphism.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})\simeq\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})
,
we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{i\in\Delta}\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})}(f_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F},f_{i}^{*}\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{i\in\Delta}\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})}(f_{i_{\sharp}}f_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})}(\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i\in\Delta^{op}}f_{i_{\sharp}}f_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})

for all
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\mathscr{T}(X)
.
Hence we obtain the desired natural isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 2.2.3
.

Let
\{f_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{X}\}_{i\in I}
be a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
producing a dividing Nisnevich covering sieve.
Then the family of functors
\{f_{i}^{*}\}_{i\in I}
is conservative.

Proof.

Assume that
\alpha
is a morphism in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})
such that
f_{i}^{*}\alpha
is an isomorphism for all
i\in I
.
This implies that the image of
\alpha
in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i_{1}}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{i_{n}})
is an isomorphism for all
i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\in I
.
Together with the equivalence (
2.2.1
),
we deduce that
\alpha
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 2.2.4
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
Then
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
.

Proof.

By
[
18
, Proposition 6.4]
,
there exists a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

such that
g
is a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
and
h_{S}\to\mathcal{S}
and
h_{X}\to h_{S}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}
are representable Zariski covers.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{X}
be Čech nerves of
h_{S}\to\mathcal{S}
and
h_{X}\to\mathcal{X}
, and let
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{S}_{i}
be the induced morphism for every integer
i\geq 0
.

We claim that
f_{i}
is a representable log smooth morphism.
The claim is trivial if
i=0
.
Assume
i>0
.
Then
f_{i}
admits a factorization

\mathcal{X}_{i}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{X}_{i-1}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{X}_{0}\to\mathcal{X}_{i-1}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{0}\to\mathcal{S}_{i-1}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{0}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{S}_{i}.

The second arrow is a representable Zariski cover since
\mathcal{X}_{0}\to\mathcal{S}_{0}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}
is a representable Zariski cover,
and the third arrow is a representable log smooth morphism by induction.
This completes the induction argument.
As a consequence,
we see that
f_{i}^{*}
preserves limits since
f_{i}\simeq h_{g_{i}}
for some log smooth morphism
g_{i}
in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
.

Consider the induced diagram

(2.2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}^{*}}

We know that the vertical arrows in (
2.2.3
) preserve limits.
Take limits along the rows in (
2.2.3
) and use the fact that the inclusion
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{R}}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
preserves limits
[
10
, Theorem 5.5.3.18]
to conclude that
f^{*}
is in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{R}}
, i.e.,
f^{*}
preserves limits.
∎

Lemma 2.2.5
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{X^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

be a cartesian square in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
with
S,S^{\prime},X,X^{\prime}\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
If
f
is a representable log smooth morphism,
then the natural transformation

(2.2.4)

f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}g^{*}f_{\sharp}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By
[
18
, Lemmas 3.4, 3.5]
,
there exists a commutative diagram with vertical isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{U^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{v}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

such that
u
is a log smooth morphism and
v
is a morphism in
\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
We conclude by log smooth base change for
\mathscr{T}
over
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
∎

Lemma 2.2.6
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
be a representable log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
and let
h_{X}\to\mathcal{X}
be a representable Zariski cover with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Form the Čech nerve
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{X}
of
h_{X}\to\mathcal{X}
,
and set
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}:=\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{Y}
.
Let
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{Y}_{i}\to\mathcal{X}_{i}
be the induced morphism for every integer
i\geq 0
.
Then there is an induced commutative diagram with horizontal equivalences

(2.2.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proof.

By Theorem
A.2.1
, Proposition
2.2.4
, and Lemma
2.2.5
, the induced functor

\mathscr{T}\colon(h(\mathbf{lFan}/B))^{op}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}

can be extended to a functor

\mathscr{T}\colon\mathrm{Corr}(h(\mathbf{lFan}/B),h(\mathbf{lSm}),\mathrm{all})\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}.

Together with the diagram
\Delta^{op}\times\Delta^{1}\to h(\mathbf{lFan}/B)
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}

we obtain a diagram
\Delta\times\Delta^{1}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1\sharp}}

Take limits along the rows to obtain a functor

\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i\sharp}\colon\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{i})\to\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i}).

Let
p_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{X}
and
q_{i}\colon\mathcal{Y}_{i}\to\mathcal{Y}
be the induced morphisms in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
and let
\alpha\colon\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})\to\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})
and
\beta\colon\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y})\to\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{i})
be the induced equivalences.
Then we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(X)}(\alpha^{-1}\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i\sharp}\beta\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\lim_{i\in\Delta}\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})}(\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i\sharp}\beta\mathcal{G},\alpha\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{i\in\Delta}\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X}_{i})}(f_{i\sharp}q_{i}^{*}\mathcal{G},p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\lim_{i\in\Delta}\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Y}_{i})}(q_{i}^{*}\mathcal{G},q_{i}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(Y)}(\mathcal{G},f^{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(X)}(f_{\sharp}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})

for all
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{T}(X)
and
\mathcal{G}\in\mathscr{T}(Y)
,
which shows the desired commutativity.
∎

Proposition 2.2.7
.

For every cartesian square in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

such that
f\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
,
the natural transformation

(2.2.6)

f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}g^{*}f_{\sharp}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(I)
Reduction of
f
.
There exists a representable Zariski cover
u\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{X}
such that
fu
is a representable log smooth morphism.
Consider the induced cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}

Assume that the natural transformations

u^{*}g_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{Ex}g_{*}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime*}\text{ and }(fu)^{*}g_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}g_{*}^{\prime\prime}(f^{\prime}u^{\prime})^{*}

are isomorphisms.
The commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}f^{*}g_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}g_{*}^{\prime}f^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}^{\prime\prime}u^{\prime*}f^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(fu)^{*}g_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{*}^{\prime\prime}(f^{\prime}u^{\prime})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

shows that
u^{*}f^{*}g_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}u^{*}g_{*}^{\prime}f^{\prime*}
is an isomorphism.
Since
u^{*}
is conservative by Proposition
2.2.3
,
we see that (
2.2.6
) is an isomorphism.
Hence we reduce to the case when
f
is a representable log smooth morphism.

(II)
Reduction of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
.
There exists a representable Zariski cover
p\colon\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}\simeq h_{S^{\prime\prime}}
for some
S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Consider the induced cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Assume that the natural transformations

f_{\sharp}^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}p^{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}\text{ and }f_{\sharp}^{\prime\prime}(g^{\prime}p^{\prime})^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}(gp)^{*}f_{\sharp}

are isomorphisms.
The commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime*}g^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}g^{*}f_{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{\prime\prime}(g^{\prime}p^{\prime})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gp)^{*}f_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

shows that the natural transformation
p^{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}p^{*}g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is an isomorphism.
Since
p^{*}
is conservative by Proposition
2.2.3
,
we see that (
2.2.6
) is an isomorphism.
Hence we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\simeq h_{S^{\prime}}
for some
S^{\prime}\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.

(III)
Reduction of
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{0}\to\mathcal{S}
be a representable Zariski cover such that
\mathcal{S}_{0}\simeq h_{S_{0}}
for some
S_{0}\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
,
and let
\mathcal{S}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{S}
be its Čech nerve.
For every integer
i\geq 0
,
consider the induced cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{i}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{i},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{i}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{i}}

where
\mathcal{S}_{i}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{i}
,

\mathcal{X}_{i}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{i}
,
and
\mathcal{X}_{i}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{i}
.
By Proposition
2.1.3
and Lemma
2.2.5
,
the natural transformation

f_{i\sharp}^{\prime}g_{i}^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}g_{i}^{*}f_{i\sharp}

is an isomorphism.
Apply
\lim_{i\in\Delta}
to this and use
[
9
, Lemma 4.3.7]
to see that the natural transformation

\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i_{\sharp}}^{\prime}\lim_{i\in\Delta}g_{i}^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}\lim_{i\in\Delta}g_{i}^{*}\lim_{i\in\Delta}f_{i\sharp}

is an isomorphism.
Together with the commutative squares (
2.2.2
) and (
2.2.5
),
we see that (
2.2.6
) is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 2.2.8
.

For every log smooth morphism
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
the natural transformation

f_{\sharp}((-)\otimes f^{*}(-))\xrightarrow{Ex}f_{\sharp}(-)\otimes(-)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
p\colon h_{X}\to\mathcal{X}
be a representable Zariski cover with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
,
and let
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\to\mathcal{X}
be its Čech nerve.
Then we have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}(p_{i_{\sharp}}p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}p_{i_{\sharp}}p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}p_{i_{\sharp}}(p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes p_{i}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(fp_{i})_{\sharp}(p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes(fp_{i})^{*}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(fp_{i})_{\sharp}p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

for all
i\in\Delta
,
where
p_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{X}
is the induced morphism.
If we show the claim for
p_{i}
and
fp_{i}
,
then take
\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{i\in\Delta^{op}}
to the upper horizontal arrow and use Proposition
2.2.2
to conclude.
Hence we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{X}=h_{X}
with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.

There exists a representable Zariski cover
g\colon h_{S}\to\mathcal{S}
with
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Form the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}f_{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{\prime}(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{\prime*}g^{*}\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}(f_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}f_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\mathcal{F}\otimes g^{*}\mathcal{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

for all
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mathcal{G}\in\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
.
By Propositions
2.1.3
and
2.2.7
,
the upper left and lower right horizontal arrows are isomorphisms.
Proposition
2.1.5
implies that

f^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
h_{u}
for some log smooth morphism
u\colon U\to S
.
The log smooth projection formula implies that
the right vertical arrow is an isomorphism.
Hence the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism.
To conclude,
observe that
g^{*}
is conservative by Proposition
2.2.3
.
∎

Proposition 2.2.9
.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathcal{S}
be the projection.
Then
p^{*}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

There exists a representable Zariski cover
g\colon h_{S}\to\mathcal{S}
with
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Consider the induced cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

We have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}^{\prime}p^{\prime*}g^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}p^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}p_{\sharp}p^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

By
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance,
the diagonal arrow is an isomorphism.
Proposition
2.2.7
implies that the upper right horizontal arrow is an isomorphism.
Hence the right vertical arrow is an isomorphism.
To conclude,
observe that
g^{*}
is conservative by Proposition
2.2.3
.
∎

Proposition 2.2.10
.

For every closed immersion
i\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{S}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
with its open complement
j\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{S}
,
i_{*}
is fully faithful, and the pair of functors
(i^{*},j^{*})
is conservative.

Proof.

Proposition
2.1.6
implies that
i
is a representable closed immersion.
By Propositions
2.2.3
and
2.2.7
, the question is Zariski local on
\mathcal{S}
.
Hence we may assume that
\mathcal{S}=h_{S}
for some
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
and
i\simeq h_{a}
for some strict closed immersion
a\colon Z\to S
.
Then use the localization property for
\mathscr{T}
over
\mathbf{lSch}/B
to conclude.
∎

Remark 2.2.11
.

One can argue as in
[
6
, Lemma 2.3.5]
to show that the localization property for a closed immersion
i\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{S}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
is equivalent to requiring that
i_{*}
is conservative and the sequence

j_{\sharp}j^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}{\rm id}\xrightarrow{ad}i_{*}i^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.
A similar remark applies to the localization property for a strict closed immersion in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
too.

For
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
, we set

\mathcal{F}(1)[2]:=\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathrm{cofib}(M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{M_{\mathcal{S}}(i_{0})}M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}))\in\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S}),

where
i_{0}
is the
0
-section.

Proposition 2.2.12
.

Suppose
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathcal{S}
be the projection, and let
i\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the
0
-section. Then
p_{\sharp}i_{*}
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories, and
\mathscr{T}(S)
is stable.

Proof.

We have natural isomorphisms

p_{\sharp}i_{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq p_{\sharp}i_{*}i^{*}p^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{F}(1)[2]

for
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
,
where the second one is due to Propositions
2.2.8
and
2.2.10
.
Hence the claim is equivalent to saying that
\mathbf{1}[1]
and
\mathbf{1}(1)[2]
are
\otimes
-invertible.
Let us focus on the case of
\mathbf{1}(1)[2]
since the proofs are similar.
We only need to show that the evaluation morphism

\mathbf{1}(1)[2]\otimes\underline{\Hom}(\mathbf{1}(1)[2],\mathbf{1})\to\mathbf{1}

is an isomorphism,
where
\underline{\Hom}
denotes the internal Hom in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
.

There exists a representable Zariski cover
g\colon h_{S}\to\mathcal{S}
with
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Since
g^{*}
is conservative by Proposition
2.2.3
,
it suffices to show that the induced morphism

g^{*}\mathbf{1}(1)[2]\otimes g^{*}\underline{\Hom}(\mathbf{1}(1)[2],\mathbf{1})\to g^{*}\mathbf{1}

is an isomorphism.
By adjunction,
Proposition
2.2.8
yields a natural isomorphism.

g^{*}\underline{\Hom}(\mathbf{1}(1)[2],\mathbf{1})\simeq\underline{\Hom}(g^{*}\mathbf{1}(1)[2],g^{*}\mathbf{1})

Hence we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{S}=h_{S}
with
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
We finish the proof by
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stability.
∎

We summarize the results in this subsection as follows.

Theorem 2.2.13
.

The sheaf

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

satisfies the following properties:

•

For every morphism
f
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,

f_{*}
denotes a right adjoint of
f^{*}:=\mathscr{T}(f)
.

•

For every log smooth morphism
f
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
f^{*}
admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
.

•

(Log smooth base change)
For every cartesian square in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

such that
f
is log smooth, the natural transformation

f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}g^{*}f_{\sharp}

is an isomorphism.

•

(Log smooth projection formula)
For every log smooth morphism
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, the natural transformation

f_{\sharp}((-)\otimes f^{*}(-))\xrightarrow{Ex}f_{\sharp}(-)\otimes(-)

is an isomorphism.

•

(Localization property) For every closed immersion
i\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{S}
in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
with its open complement
j\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{S}
,

i_{*}
is fully faithful,
and the pair of functors
(i^{*},j^{*})
is conservative.

•

(
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance)
Suppose
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathcal{S}
be the projection.
Then
p^{*}
is fully faithful.

•

(
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stability) Suppose
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\mathcal{S}
be the projection, and let
i\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the
0
-section.
Then
p_{\sharp}i_{*}
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories, and
\mathscr{T}(S)
is stable.

2.3.
Miscellaneous properties

In this subsection, we collect several properties for
\mathscr{T}
that are needed later.

For
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
, let
\mathbf{Ft}/\underline{S}
be the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Sch}/\underline{S}
consisting of schemes of finite type over
\underline{S}
.
As observed in
[
6
, Theorem 3.4.2]
, the functor
(\mathbf{Ft}/\underline{S})^{op}\to\mathbf{Tri}^{\otimes}
sending
X\in\mathbf{Ft}/\underline{S}
to
\mathscr{T}(X\times_{\underline{S}}S)
is a motivic triangulated category in the sense of
[
6
, Definition 2.4.45]
, where
\mathbf{Tri}^{\otimes}
denotes the
2
-category of closed symmetric monoidal triangulated categories whose
1
-morphisms are the symmetric monoidal functors and whose
2
-morphisms are the symmetric monoidal natural transformations.
In particular, we have the six-functor formalism for strict morphisms, see
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.2]
for the details.

Proposition 2.3.1
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a strict separated smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
i\colon X\to S
be its section.
Then
f_{\sharp}i_{*}
is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

By the above argument, we can use
[
6
, Proposition 2.4.11]
since
i
is strict.
∎

Proposition 2.3.2
.

Let
i\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{S}
be a closed immersion in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
Then
i_{*}
admits a right adjoint
i^{!}
.

Proof.

Let
j\colon\mathcal{S}-\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{S}
be the open complement of
i
.
Let
\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}\mathcal{F}_{a}
be a colimit in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{Z})
.
We need to show that the induced morphism

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}i_{*}\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}\mathcal{F}

is an isomorphism.
Since
(i^{*},j^{*})
is conservative, it suffices to show that the induced morphisms

i^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}i_{*}\mathcal{F}\to i^{*}i_{*}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}\mathcal{F}\text{ and }j^{*}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}i_{*}\mathcal{F}\to j^{*}i_{*}\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{a\in I}\mathcal{F}

are isomorphisms.
To conclude, observe that
i^{*}
and
j^{*}
preserve colimits, and use
i^{*}i_{*}\simeq{\rm id}
and
j^{*}i_{*}\simeq 0
.
∎

Proposition 2.3.3
.

For all
X,Y\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
, there is a canonical equivalence

\mathscr{T}(X)\times\mathscr{T}(Y)\simeq\mathscr{T}(X\amalg Y).

A similar claim holds for
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
too.

Proof.

Argue as in
[
6
, Lemma 2.3.7]
.
∎

Proposition 2.3.4
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

be a cartesian square in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
If
f
is an open immersion and
g
is strict proper, then the natural transformation

Ex\colon f_{\sharp}g_{*}^{\prime}\to g_{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

This is observed in
[
17
, §3.4]
.
∎

Proposition 2.3.5
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

be a cartesian square in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
such that
i
is a closed immersion.
Then the natural transformation

(2.3.1)

Ex\colon f^{*}i_{*}\to i_{*}^{\prime}f^{\prime*}

is an isomorphism.
If
f\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
, then the natural transformation

(2.3.2)

Ex\colon f_{\sharp}i_{*}^{\prime}\to i_{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
j
is the open complement of
i
.
Use the fact that
i_{*}
and
i_{*}^{\prime}
are fully faithful to show that the natural transformation

i^{\prime*}f^{*}i_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}i^{\prime*}i_{*}^{\prime}f^{\prime*}

is an isomorphism.
Use
j^{*}i_{*}=0
and
j^{\prime*}i_{*}^{\prime}=0
to show that the natural transformation

j^{\prime*}f^{*}i_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}j^{\prime*}i_{*}^{\prime}f^{\prime*}

is an isomorphism.
Together with the fact that the pair
(i^{\prime*},j^{\prime*})
is conservative, we deduce that (
2.3.1
) is an isomorphism.

Suppose
f\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
.
By a similar argument, the natural transformations

i^{*}f_{\sharp}i_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{Ex}i^{*}i_{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}\text{ and }j^{*}f_{\sharp}i_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{Ex}j^{*}i_{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}

are isomorphisms.
Use the conservativity of
(i^{*},j^{*})
to conclude.
∎

Proposition 2.3.6

(
ver
-invariance)
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
j\colon X-\partial_{S}X\to X
be the obvious open immersion.
Then the natural transformation

f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}j_{*}j^{*}f^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the induced morphism

\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(X)}(M(V)(d)[n],f^{*}\mathcal{F})\to\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(X)}(M(V)(d)[n],j_{*}j^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{F})

is an isomorphism for all
V\in\mathbf{lSm}/X
,
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{T}(S)
, and
d,n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By adjunction, it suffices to show that the induced morphism

M_{S}(V\times_{X}(X-\partial_{S}X))\to M_{S}(V)

in
\mathscr{T}(S)
is an isomorphism.
To conclude, observe that the open immersion
V\times_{X}(X-\partial_{S}X)\to V
is in
ver_{S}
by
[
17
, Propositions 2.3.8(6), 2.3.9]
.
∎

3.
Gysin isomorphisms

Recall that we fix a sheaf

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

satisfying the conditions in the introduction.

The purpose of this section is to show that there is a natural isomorphism

M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})\simeq M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}/\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})

in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})

for every closed immersion
a\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
in
\mathbf{lSmSpc}/\mathcal{S}
with
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.
The outline of the proof is as follows.
We begin with the deformation to the normal cone construction in
[
18
]
.
Then we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{S}=h_{S}
for some
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
and
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
is equal to
h_{i}
for some strict closed immersion
i\colon Z\to X
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
.
To reduce to the known case
[
5
, Theorem 7.5.4]
, we employ a log smooth morphism
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
in §
3.3
such that
g^{*}
is conservative and the projection
X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
is saturated.
To construct such a morphism
g
, we need the content of §
3.2
, where we investigate which morphisms are inverted when we invert dividing covers and
ver
simultaneously.
This investigation requires the notion of pans in §
3.1
.
As inverting log blow-ups produces a divided log space from an fs log scheme, inverting subdivisions produces a pan from a fan.

3.1.
Pans

A fan chart of an fs log scheme is useful for understanding the local behavior.
To obtain an analogous notion of a fan chart for divided log spaces, we first need to invert all subdivisions in the category of fans.
Then only the support and the lattice matter.
This reasoning leads to the following notion.

Definition 3.1.1
.

A
pan
\mathcal{A}
is a lattice
L_{\mathcal{A}}
together with a subset
\mathcal{A}
of
L_{\mathcal{A},\mathbb{R}}:=L_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathcal{A}
is the support
\lvert\Sigma\rvert
of a fan
\Sigma
in
L_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Here, the support
\lvert\Sigma\rvert
is defined to be the set of points
x\in L_{\mathcal{A},\mathbb{R}}
such that
rx
is in a cone of
\Sigma
for some real number
r>0
.
In this case, we say that the
underlying pan of
\Sigma
is
\mathcal{A}
.

A
morphism of pans
f\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
is a homomorphism of lattices
L_{f}\colon L_{\mathcal{B}}\to L_{\mathcal{A}}
such that
L_{f,\mathbb{R}}:=L_{f}\otimes\mathbb{R}
maps
\mathcal{B}
into
\mathcal{A}
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
be the category of fans
\Sigma
in
L_{\mathcal{A}}
such that the underlying pan of
\Sigma
is
\mathcal{A}
.
If
\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
, then the fan

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}:=\{\sigma\cap\sigma^{\prime}:\sigma\in\Sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime}\}

is an object of
\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
.
This shows that
\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
is filtered.
We set

\mathbb{T}(\mathcal{A}):=\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}:=\mathop{\mathrm{lim}}_{\Sigma\in\mathcal{A}_{Fan}}h_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}\in\mathbf{Shv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lFan}/\Spec(\mathbb{Z})).

If
\Sigma^{\prime}\to\Sigma
is a morphism in
\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
, then
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
is a dividing cover.
Hence we have an isomorphism
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}\simeq h_{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}}
whenever
\Sigma\in\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
.
In particular,
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/\Spec(\mathbb{Z})
.
We set
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A},B}:=\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}\times B\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.

Let
\mathbf{Fan}
denote the category of fans, and let
\mathbf{Pan}
denote the category of pans.
We have the functor

\lvert-\rvert\colon\mathbf{Fan}\to\mathbf{Pan}

sending
\Sigma
to
\lvert\Sigma\rvert
.
Furthermore, we have the functor

\mathbb{T}\colon\mathbf{Pan}\to\mathbf{lSpc}/\Spec(\mathbb{Z})

sending
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Observe that the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Fan}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Pan}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{lSpc}/\Spec(\mathbb{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h\circ\mathbb{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lvert-\rvert}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}

commutes.

Definition 3.1.2
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
be a morphism of pans.
We say that
\mathcal{B}
is a
subpan of
\mathcal{A}
if
L_{f}\colon L_{\mathcal{B}}\to L_{\mathcal{A}}
is an isomorphism.
We say that
f
is an
isogeny
if
f
is surjective and the kernel of
L_{f}
is a finite group.

Proposition 3.1.3
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{n}
be subpans of a pan
\mathcal{A}
.
Then there exists a fan
\Sigma
such that the underlying pan of
\Sigma
is
\mathcal{A}
and
\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}_{i}
is a subfan of
\Sigma
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
.

Proof.

Choose fans
\Sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{Fan}
and
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}\in(\mathcal{B}_{i})_{Fan}
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
.
According to
[
14
, p. 18]
, there exists a fan
\Delta_{i}
such that it contains
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
as a subfan and
\lvert\Delta_{i}\rvert=L_{\mathcal{A}}
.
We set

\Sigma:=\{\sigma^{\prime}\cap\delta_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\delta_{n}:\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\prime},\delta_{1}\in\Delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n}\in\Delta_{n}\}.

This fan satisfies the condition.
∎

Proposition 3.1.4
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
be subpans of a pan
\mathcal{A}
.
Then
\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
are subpans of
\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.1.3
, there exists a fan
\Sigma
such that its underlying pan is
\mathcal{A}
and
\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}
and
\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
are subfans of
\Sigma
.
Then
\Sigma\cap(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\prime})
and
\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
are subfans of
\Sigma
, and their underlying pans are
\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.
∎

Definition 3.1.5
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{n}
are subpans of a pan
\mathcal{A}
.
We say that
\{\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{n}\}
is a
closed cover
of
\mathcal{A}
if
\mathcal{B}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{B}_{n}=\mathcal{A}
.
In this case, the associated
Čech cube
is defined to be the functor

Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathbf{Pan}

such that

Q(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}):=\mathcal{B}_{1}^{a_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{B}_{n}^{a_{n}}

for
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in(\Delta^{1})^{n}
and the morphism
Q(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\to Q(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
is the inclusion for every morphism
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\to(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
in
(\Delta^{1})^{n}
, where

\mathcal{B}_{n}^{a_{n}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{B}_{n}&\text{if }a_{n}=0,\\
\mathcal{A}&\text{if }a_{n}=1.\end{array}\right.

For the notion of the total cofibers of cubes, we refer to
[
4
, §A.6]
.
A cube in a stable
\infty
-category is
cocartesian
if its total cofiber is isomorphic to
0
.

Proposition 3.1.6
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{n}
be subpans of
\mathcal{A}
such that
\{\mathcal{B}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{n}\}
is a closed cover of
\mathcal{A}
, and let
Q
be the associated Čech cube.
Then the cube

M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{T}(Q)\times\mathcal{S})

in
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
is cocartesian for all
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.1.3
, there exists a fan
\Sigma
such that the underlying pan of
\Sigma
is
\mathcal{A}
and
\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}_{i}
is a subfan of
\Sigma
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
.
Then
\{\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma\cap\mathcal{B}_{n}}\}
is a Zariski cover of
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}
, so
\{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{B}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{B}_{n}}\}
is a Zariski cover of
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
We conclude by dividing Nisnevich descent.
∎

3.2.
Localizations of monoids by critical faces

We invert all morphisms belonging to
ver
.
Since vertical boundaries are not stable under dividing covers, inverting both dividing covers and
ver
leads to inverting more morphisms.
For example, consider the diagonal homomorphism
\theta\colon\mathbb{N}\to P:=\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{N}
and the face
F:=0\oplus\mathbb{N}
of
P
.
We have the following cones in
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}:=\mathrm{Cone}((1,0),(1,1)),\;\sigma_{2}:=\mathrm{Cone}((1,1),(0,1)),

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}:=\mathrm{Cone}((1,0),(0,1)),\;\sigma_{4}:=\mathrm{Cone}((1,0)).

Form the four fans
\Sigma_{1},\ldots,\Sigma_{4}
whose sets of maximal cones are
\{\sigma_{1}\}
,
\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\}
,
\{\sigma_{3}\}
, and
\{\sigma_{4}\}
.
We have morphisms of fans
\Sigma_{1}\to\Sigma_{2}\to\Sigma_{3}\leftarrow\Sigma_{4}
as illustrated in the following figure.

\xrightarrow{ver}
\Sigma_{1}
\xrightarrow{div}
\Sigma_{2}
\xleftarrow{ver}
\Sigma_{3}
\Sigma_{4}

Since
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{2}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{3}}
is a dividing cover and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{1}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{2}}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{4}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma_{3}}
are in
ver_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}
, we deduce that the morphism

M_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}(\mathbb{A}_{P_{F}})\to M_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}(\mathbb{A}_{P})

induced by the open immersion
\mathbb{A}_{P_{F}}\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
is an isomorphism.

The main result of this section is Theorem
3.2.4
, which provides a class of morphisms that are inverted in
\mathscr{T}
by similar ways.

For an fs monoid
P
such that
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free, let
\Spec(P)
denote the fan with the single maximal cone
P^{\vee}
, where
P^{\vee}
denotes the dual monoid of
P
in the dual lattice
(P^{\mathrm{gp}})^{\vee}
.

Definition 3.2.1
.

A pan
\mathcal{A}
is
strongly convex
if
\mathcal{A}
is convex and
\mathcal{A}\cap(-\mathcal{A})=0
.
In this case,
\mathcal{A}
is a cone, so we can discuss the faces of
\mathcal{A}
.
Furthermore, we have
\mathcal{A}\simeq\lvert\Spec(P)\rvert
for some fs monoid
P
such that
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

Let
\theta\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{A}
a morphism of strongly convex pans, and let
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
be the union of faces
\mathcal{V}
of
\mathcal{B}
such that
\theta
maps any nontrivial face of
\mathcal{V}
to a nontrivial subpan of
\mathcal{A}
.
We regard
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
as a subpan of
\mathcal{B}
.
If
\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A},B}
is a morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
and the projection
\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathcal{X}
is log smooth, then the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{B}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The question is Zariski local on
\mathcal{X}
.
Hence we may assume
\mathcal{X}=h_{X}
for some
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
and
X\to\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A},B}
is equal to
h_{p}
for some morphism
p\colon X\to\mathbb{A}_{P,B}
, where
P
is a sharp fs monoid and
\mathbb{A}_{P,B}:=\mathbb{A}_{P}\times B
.
We may also assume that
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}
is equal to
h_{\mathbb{A}_{\theta}}\colon h_{\mathbb{A}_{Q}}\to h_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}
for some homomorphism
\eta\colon P\to Q
of sharp fs monoids.

Let
\Sigma
be the subfan of
\Spec(Q)
whose underlying pan is
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.
By
[
17
, Proposition 2.3.13]
, the open immersion
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is contained in
ver_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}
.
Together with
[
17
, Proposition 2.3.9]
, we obtain an isomorphism

(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q})-\partial_{X}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q})\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}.

Hence the induced morphism

M_{X}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma})\to M_{X}(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q})

is an isomorphism by
ver
-invariance, which gives the desired isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 3.2.3
.

Suppose
\mathcal{X}:=h_{X}
with
X\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
strongly convex pans, let
\theta\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}
be an isogeny of pans such that the torsion part of the kernel of
L_{\mathcal{C}}\to L_{\mathcal{A}}
is invertible in
X
, and let
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
be elements of the dual lattice
L_{\mathcal{C}}^{\vee}
.
We set

\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\geq 0}:=\{y\in\mathcal{C}:\langle y,x_{i}\rangle\geq 0\},\;\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\leq 0}:=\{y\in\mathcal{C}:\langle y,x_{i}\rangle\leq 0\},\;\mathcal{H}_{i}:=\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\geq 0}\cap\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\leq 0}

for all
i=1,\ldots,n
.
We assume
\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)\subset\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\geq 0}
for all
i=1,\ldots,n
.
If
\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A},B}
is a morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
then the induced map

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.
Observe that
\mathcal{C}
is a strongly convex pan.
Every face of
\mathcal{C}
can be written as
\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G})
for some faces
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{G}
of
\mathcal{B}
.
Furthermore,
\theta
maps any nontrivial face of
\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G})
to a nontrivial subpan of
\mathcal{A}
if and only if
\mathcal{G}=0
.
Hence Lemma
3.2.2
shows that the induced morphism

(3.2.1)

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{C}})

is an isomorphism.
In particular, the claim holds for
n=0
.

Assume
n>0
.
Let
Q
be the Čech cube
(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathbf{Pan}
associated with the closed cover

\{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0},\ldots,\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}\}

of
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}
.
As a consequence of Proposition
3.1.6
,
the cube
M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}(Q))
is cocartesian.
Furthermore, the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{i_{1}}^{\geq 0}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{H}_{i_{r}}^{\geq 0}})

is an isomorphism for every subset
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}\subsetneq\{1,\ldots,n\}
by induction.
Hence to show that the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}})=M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{Q(0,\ldots,0)})

is an isomorphism, it suffices to show that the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}})=M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{Q(1,\ldots,1)})

is an isomorphism.

Every face of the convex pan
\mathcal{H}^{\leq 0}:=\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\leq 0}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\leq 0}
not contained in

\mathcal{H}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\leq 0}\cap\cdots\cap\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\leq 0}\cap\mathcal{H}_{i})

is of the form
\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G})\cap\mathcal{H}^{\leq 0}
for some faces
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{G}
of
\mathcal{B}
.
By the assumption
\theta^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)\subset\mathcal{H}_{i}^{\geq 0}
for all
i
, we see that
\mathcal{G}\neq 0
in this case.
Furthermore,
\theta
maps
\theta^{-1}(0\times\mathcal{G})\cap\mathcal{H}^{\leq 0}
to the trivial subpan of
\mathcal{A}
.
Hence Lemma
3.2.2
shows that the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}^{\leq 0}})

is an isomorphism.
Since

\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{\leq 0}\cap(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0})\text{ and }\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{H}^{\leq 0}\cup(\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}),

the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\geq 0}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\geq 0}})\to M_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{C}})

is an isomorphism by Proposition
3.1.6
.
Combine with (
3.2.1
) to finish the proof.
∎

We refer to
[
15
, Definition I.4.2.10]
for the notion of
\theta
-critical faces when
\theta
is a homomorphism of monoids.

Theorem 3.2.4
.

Let
X\to\mathbb{A}_{P,B}:=\mathbb{A}_{P}\times B
be a morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, where
P
is a sharp fs monoid.
If
\theta\colon P\to Q
is an injective, local, and locally exact homomorphism of fs monoids such that the torsion part of
\theta^{\mathrm{gp}}
is invertible in
X
, then the natural transformation

f_{\sharp}j_{\sharp}j^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}f^{*}

is an equivalence for all
\theta
-critical face
G
of
Q
, where
f\colon X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}\to X
is the projection, and
j\colon X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q_{G}}\to X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q}
is the obvious open immersion.

Proof.

Observe that
f
is log smooth by
[
15
, Theorem IV.3.1.8]
.
We need to show that the induced morphism

(3.2.2)

M_{X}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q_{G}})\to M_{X}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q})

is an isomorphism for all
X^{\prime}\in\mathbf{lSm}/X
.

Assume first that
G
is a maximal
\theta
-critical face of
Q
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathcal{D}
be the underlying pans of
\Spec(P)
,
\Spec(G)
, and
\Spec(Q)
.
The summation homomorphism
P\oplus G\to Q
gives a morphism of pans
\alpha\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}
.
We set
\mathcal{C}:=L_{\mathcal{D}}\times_{L_{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}}}(\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B})
, which contains
\mathcal{D}
as a subpan.
By
[
15
, Theorem I.4.7.7(2)]
, the induced homomorphism
P\to Q/G
is exact.
The implication (4)
\Rightarrow
(3) in
[
15
, Lemma I.4.7.9]
shows that the induced morphism
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}
is an isogeny.
Choose elements
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in Q-P
such that
P\cup\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
generates
Q
.
Then we have an equality

\mathcal{D}=\{y\in\mathcal{C}:\langle y,x_{i}\rangle\geq 0\;\;\forall i=1,\ldots,n\}.

Furthermore,
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{A}\times 0)
is the underlying pan of
\Spec(Q_{G})
.
Use Lemma
3.2.3
to see that (
3.2.2
) is an isomorphism.

For general
G
, let
G^{\prime}
be a maximal
\theta
-critical face of
Q
containing
G
.
The induced morphisms
