M_{X}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q_{G^{\prime}}})\to M_{X}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q})\text{ and }M_{X}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q_{G^{\prime}}})\to M_{X}(X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{Q_{G}})

are isomorphisms by what we have proven for maximal
\theta
-critical faces.
We conclude by 2-out-of-3.
∎

3.3.
Saturations of morphisms

We refer to
[
20
, Definitions I.3.12, II.2.10]
and
[
15
, Definitions I.4.8.2, III.2.5.1]
for the notions of saturated homomorphisms of integral monoids and saturated morphisms of integral log schemes.
The purpose of this subsection is to show that we can make a morphism of fs log schemes into a saturated morphism after a base change along a suitable log smooth morphism whose pullback functor of motives is conservative.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
and
G\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{E}
be functors of categories.
Assume that for all
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
, the function

\Hom_{\mathcal{D}}(FX,FY)\rightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{E}}(GFX,GFY)

given by
f\mapsto Gf
is bijective.
If
F
is conservative, then
GF
is also conservative.

Proof.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
\mathcal{C}
such that
GFf
is an isomorphism.
There exists a unique morphism
g\colon FY\to FX
such that
Gg
is an inverse of
GFf
by the bijectivity.
Since
G(Ff\circ g)={\rm id}
and
G(g\circ Ff)={\rm id}
, we see that
g
is an inverse of
Ff
by the bijectivity.
The conservativity of
F
implies that
f
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 3.3.2
.

For homomorphisms of saturated monoids
P\to Q,Q^{\prime}
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\overline{Q\oplus_{P}Q^{\prime}}\simeq\overline{\overline{Q}\oplus_{\overline{P}}\overline{Q^{\prime}}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{Mon}
denote the category of saturated monoids.
For a saturated sharp monoid
M
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(Q\oplus_{P}Q^{\prime},M)\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(Q,M)\times_{\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(P,M)}\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(Q^{\prime},M)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(\overline{Q},M)\times_{\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(\overline{P},M)}\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(\overline{Q^{\prime}},M)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathbf{Mon}}(\overline{Q}\oplus_{\overline{P}}\overline{Q},M).

We conclude together with the fact that the quotient homomorphism
(-)\to\overline{(-)}
is the universal homomorphism to a sharp saturated monoid.
∎

Proposition 3.3.3
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be an exact morphism of fs log schemes.
Then there exists a Kummer log smooth morphism
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
such that
g^{*}
is conservative and the projection
f^{\prime}\colon X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
is saturated.

Proof.

The question is Zariski local on
S
by Proposition
2.2.3
.
Hence we may assume that
S
admits a neat chart
P
.
The question is also Zariski local on
X
.
We may also assume that there exists a chart
\theta\colon P\to Q
of
f
such that
\theta
is locally exact.
By
[
15
, Theorem I.4.9.1]
, there exists an integer
n>0
such that the pushout of
\theta
along
P\xrightarrow{\cdot n}P
in the category of saturated monoids is saturated.
Consider the injective and local homomorphisms

P\to P^{\prime}:=P^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus P\text{ and }\eta\colon P\to P^{\prime\prime}:=P\oplus P

sending
p\in P
to
(p,np)
.
Use these homomorphisms to define

S^{\prime}:=S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{P^{\prime}}\text{ and }S^{\prime\prime}:=S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{A}_{P^{\prime\prime}}.

We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where
g
and
h
are the projections, and
s
(resp.
j
) is induced by the first projection
P^{\prime\prime}\to P
(resp. the inclusion
P^{\prime\prime}\to P^{\prime}
).

Since
s^{*}h^{*}\simeq{\rm id}
,
h^{*}
is conservative.
The only maximal
\eta
-critical faces of
P^{\prime\prime}
are
P\oplus 0
and
0\oplus P
.
By the implication (1)
\Leftrightarrow
(5) in
[
15
, Theorem I.4.7.7]
,
\eta
is locally exact.
Apply Theorem
3.2.4
to
\eta
and its critical face
P\oplus 0
to obtain an isomorphism
g_{\sharp}g^{*}\simeq h_{\sharp}h^{*}
.
Together with Lemma
3.3.1
, we see that
g^{*}
is conservative.

Combine
[
15
, Proposition I.4.8.5(4)]
and Lemma
3.3.2
to show that the pushout of
\theta
along
P\to P^{\prime}
is saturated too.
Use
[
20
, Lemma II.3.5]
to deduce that the projection
f^{\prime}
is saturated.
The cokernel of
P^{\mathrm{gp}}\to P^{\prime\mathrm{gp}}
is isomorphic to
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
, which is torsion free since
P
is sharp.
Together with
[
15
, Theorem IV.3.1.8]
, we see that
g
is Kummer log smooth.
∎

Proposition 3.3.4
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a morphism of fs log schemes.
Then there exists a log smooth morphism
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
such that
g^{*}
is conservative and the projection
f^{\prime}\colon X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
is saturated.

Proof.

By
[
17
, Proposition 4.2.3]
, there exists a dividing Zariski cover
u\colon U\to S
such that the projection
X\times_{S}U\to U
is exact.
Proposition
2.2.3
shows that
u^{*}
is conservative.
Apply Proposition
3.3.3
to this projection to conclude.
∎

For a morphism of fs log schemes
f\colon X\to S
,
we set

\mathcal{M}_{X/S}:=\mathrm{coker}(\mathcal{M}_{\underline{X}\times_{\underline{S}}S}\to\mathcal{M}_{X}),

see
[
15
, Definition II.1.1.10(2)]
.

Remark 3.3.5
.

The projection
f^{\prime}
in Propositions
3.3.3
and
3.3.4
is saturated, and this implies that
\mathcal{M}_{X\times_{S}S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free by
[
15
, Theorem IV.2.5.5]
.

3.4.
Deformation to the normal cone

Throughout this subsection, we fix
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
and a closed immersion
a\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
in
\mathbf{lSmSpc}/S
.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
be the structure morphism.
From
[
18
, Definition 9.13]
, we have the commutative diagram of the deformation to the normal cone

(3.4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}

where
e
is the projection,
i_{0}
is the
0
-section,
i_{1}
is the
1
-section.

Theorem 3.4.1
.

With the above notation, the natural morphisms

M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})\to M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}/\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z}\times\square)\leftarrow M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}/\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The question is Zariski local on
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{X}
.
Hence as in the proof of
[
18
, Theorem 9.12]
, we may assume that
\mathcal{S}=h_{S}
for some
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
and
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
is equal to
h_{i}
for some strict closed immersion
i\colon Z\to X
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
.
Then it suffices to show that the induced morphisms

(3.4.2)

M_{S}(X/X-Z)\to M_{S}(\mathrm{D}_{Z}X/\mathrm{D}_{Z}X-Z\times\square)\leftarrow M_{S}(\mathrm{N}_{Z}X/\mathrm{N}_{Z}X-Z)

are isomorphisms.

By Proposition
3.3.4
and Remark
3.3.5
, there exists a log smooth morphism
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
such that
g^{*}
is conservative and
\mathcal{M}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free, where
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
.
If we apply
g^{*}
to (
3.4.2
), then we get

M_{S^{\prime}}(X^{\prime}/X^{\prime}-Z^{\prime})\to M_{S^{\prime}}(\mathrm{D}_{Z^{\prime}}X^{\prime}/\mathrm{D}_{Z^{\prime}}X^{\prime}-Z^{\prime}\times\square)\leftarrow M_{S^{\prime}}(\mathrm{N}_{Z^{\prime}}X^{\prime}/\mathrm{N}_{Z^{\prime}}X^{\prime}-Z^{\prime})

by
[
18
, Lemma D.3]
.
Hence we can replace
S
by
S^{\prime}
to assume that
\mathcal{M}_{X/S}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free.

Then
[
18
, Proposition C.1]
allows us to assume that there exists
Y\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
and a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbb{A}^{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

such that
i_{0}
is the zero section and
u
is strict étale.
Observe that both
X
and
Z
are strict smooth over
Y
.
We can replace
S
by
Y
to assume that
X
and
Z
are strict smooth over
S
.

Let
p\colon S\to\underline{S}
be the canonical morphism.
Combine
[
5
, Theorem 7.5.4]
and
ver
-invariance to obtain natural isomorphisms

M_{\underline{S}}(\underline{X}/\underline{X}-\underline{Z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}M_{\underline{S}}(\mathrm{D}_{\underline{Z}}\underline{X}/\mathrm{D}_{\underline{Z}}\underline{X}-\underline{Z}\times\square)\xleftarrow{\simeq}M_{\underline{S}}(\mathrm{N}_{\underline{Z}}\underline{X}/\mathrm{N}_{\underline{Z}}\underline{X}-\underline{Z}).

Apply the functor
p^{*}
to these to conclude.
∎

Definition 3.4.2
.

With the above notation, the
Gysin isomorphism
is defined to be the natural isomorphism

M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})\simeq M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}/\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{Z})

obtained by Theorem
3.4.1
.

4.
Thom transformations

Recall that we fix a sheaf

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

satisfying the conditions in the introduction.
In §
4.1
, we define the Thom transformation
\mathrm{Th}
and its variants
\mathrm{Th}_{d}
and
\mathrm{Th}_{n}
.
We use the Gysin isomorphisms to show
\mathrm{Th}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Th}_{d}\xleftarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Th}_{n}
.
In §
4.2
and §
4.3
, we study various functorial properties of Thom transformations as in
[
3
, §1.5.2]
.

4.1.
Application of the Gysin isomorphisms

Throughout this subsection,
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbf{lSpc}/B
,
p\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
is a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
, and
a\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{X}
is its section.
We assume that
a
is a closed immersion.
From (
3.4.1
), we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}

where
e
is the projection,
i_{0}
is the
0
-section, and
i_{1}
is the
1
-section.

Definition 4.1.1
.

We set

\displaystyle\mathrm{Th}(p,a):=p_{\sharp}a_{*},\;\mathrm{Th}_{d}(p,a):=(ep_{d})_{\sharp}a_{d*}e^{*},\;\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a):=\mathrm{Th}(p_{n},a_{n}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a):=a^{!}p^{*},\;\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a):=e_{*}a_{d}^{!}(ep_{d})^{*},\;\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a):=\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p_{n},a_{n}),

where
a^{!}
,
a_{d}^{!}
, and
a_{n}^{!}
are right adjoints of
a_{*}
,
a_{d*}
, and
a_{n*}
, which exist by Proposition
2.3.2
.
Let
T_{d}\colon\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)\to\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)
be the natural transformation given by the composition

e_{*}a_{d}^{!}(ep_{d})^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}e_{*}i_{1*}i_{1}^{*}a_{d}^{!}(ep_{d})^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}e_{*}i_{1*}a^{!}l_{1}^{*}(ep_{d})^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}a^{!}p^{*}.

We define
T_{n}\colon\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)\to\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)
similarly.
By adjunction, we obtain the natural transformations

T_{d}\colon\mathrm{Th}(p,a)\to\mathrm{Th}_{d}(p,a)\text{ and }T_{n}\colon\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)\to\mathrm{Th}_{d}(p,a).

Proposition 4.1.2
.

The natural transformations
T_{d}
and
T_{n}
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

We focus on
T_{d}
since the proof for
T_{n}
is similar.
We only need to show that the induced morphism

\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})}(M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})(d)[n],\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)\mathcal{F})\to\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})}(M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})(d)[n],\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)\mathcal{F})

is an isomorphism for all
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})
,
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}/\mathcal{S}
, and
d,n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By adjunction and the localization property, it suffices to show that the induced morphism

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})}(M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}-\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times\square)(d)[n],\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{S})}(M_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}^{\prime}-\mathcal{S}^{\prime})(d)[n],\mathcal{F})

is an isomorphism, where
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
.
Theorem
3.4.1
shows that the induced morphism

M_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}^{\prime}-\mathcal{S}^{\prime})\to M_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}-\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times\square)

is an isomorphism.
Apply
q_{\sharp}
to this to conclude, where
q\colon\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{S}
is the structure morphism.
∎

Definition 4.1.3
.

Let
T\colon\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)
be the composition

\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)\xrightarrow{T_{n}^{-1}}\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)\xrightarrow{T_{d}}\mathrm{Th}(p,a).

By adjunction, we obtain
T\colon\mathrm{Th}(p,a)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)
.

4.2.
Exchange transformations

Throughout this subsection, we fix a commutative diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

in
\mathbf{lSpc}/B
such that
p
and
p^{\prime}
are log smooth,
a
and
a^{\prime}
are closed immersions,
pa={\rm id}
, and
p^{\prime}a^{\prime}={\rm id}
.

Construction 4.2.1
.

We have the natural transformation

(4.2.1)

Ex\colon u^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)\to\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}

given by the composition

u^{*}a^{!}p^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}a^{\prime!}v^{*}p^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}u^{*},

where the first arrow is defined by Proposition
2.3.5
.
Observe that if
u\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
, then the first arrow is an isomorphism, and hence (
4.2.1
) is an isomorphism.

If
u\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
, then a left adjoint of (
4.2.1
) is

(4.2.2)

Ex\colon u_{\sharp}\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Th}(p,a)u_{\sharp}.

Construction 4.2.2
.

We have the natural isomorphism

(4.2.3)

Ex\colon u_{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)u_{*}

given by the composition

u_{*}a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex}a^{!}v_{*}p^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{Ex^{-1}}a^{!}p^{*}u_{*},

where the first arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition
2.3.5
, and the second arrow is defined and an isomorphism by log smooth base change.

A left adjoint of (
4.2.3
) is

(4.2.4)

Ex\colon u^{*}\mathrm{Th}(p,a)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}.

Construction 4.2.3
.

We have the natural transformation

(4.2.5)

Ex\colon\mathrm{Th}(p,a)u_{*}\to u_{*}\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})

given by the composition

p_{\sharp}a_{*}u_{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}p_{\sharp}v_{*}a_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{Ex}u_{*}p_{\sharp}^{\prime}.

Proposition 4.2.4
.

The functor
\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

We need to show that the natural transformations

{\rm id}\xrightarrow{ad}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p_{n},a_{n})\mathrm{Th}(p_{n},a_{n})\text{ and }\mathrm{Th}(p_{n},a_{n})\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p_{n},a_{n})\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}{\rm id}

are isomorphisms.
By
[
18
, Proposition 9.19]
, we have a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S\times\mathbb{A}^{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with
S\in\mathbf{lFan}/B
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
g
is a Zariski cover and
q
is the projection.
Let
b\colon S\to S\times\mathbb{A}^{n}
be the
0
-section.

By Proposition
2.2.3
and Constructions
4.2.1
and
4.2.2
, we reduce to showing that the natural transformations

g^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(h_{q},h_{b})\mathrm{Th}(h_{q},h_{b})g^{*}\text{ and }\mathrm{Th}(h_{q},h_{b})\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(h_{q},h_{b})g^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}g^{*}

are isomorphisms.
Hence it suffices to show that
\mathrm{Th}(h_{q},h_{b})
is an equivalence of categories.
This follows from Proposition
2.3.1
.
∎

Theorem 4.2.5
.

The functor
\mathrm{Th}(p,a)
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

Combine Propositions
4.1.2
and
4.2.4
.
∎

Since
\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)
is a right adjoint of
\mathrm{Th}(p,a)
, Theorem
4.2.5
shows that
\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)
is an inverse of
\mathrm{Th}(p,a)
.

Proposition 4.2.6
.

The square

(4.2.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{Th}(p,a)u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}\mathrm{Th}(p,a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

commutes.

Proof.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}a^{!}p^{*}p_{\sharp}a_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{!}v^{*}p^{*}p_{\sharp}a_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}u^{*}p_{\sharp}a_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}a^{!}a_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime!}v^{*}a_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}p_{\sharp}^{\prime}v^{*}a_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime!}a_{*}^{\prime}u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}p_{\sharp}^{\prime}a_{*}^{\prime}u^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

commutes, which shows the claim.
∎

Proposition 4.2.7
.

The natural transformation (
4.2.1
) is an isomorphism.
If
u\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
, then the natural transformation (
4.2.2
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The left vertical and upper horizontal arrows of (
4.2.6
) are isomorphisms by Theorem
4.2.5
.
The lower horizontal arrow of (
4.2.6
) is an isomorphism, see (
4.2.4
).
Hence the right vertical arrow of (
4.2.6
) is an isomorphism.
Theorem
4.2.5
finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.2.8
.

The square

(4.2.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(p,a)u_{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

commutes.

Proof.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}a_{*}u_{*}a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}a_{*}a^{!}v_{*}p^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}a_{*}a^{!}p^{*}u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}v_{*}a_{*}^{\prime}a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}v_{*}p^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sharp}p^{*}u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}p_{\sharp}^{\prime}a_{*}^{\prime}a^{\prime!}p^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}p_{\sharp}^{\prime}p^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

commutes, which shows the claim.
∎

Proposition 4.2.9
.

The natural transformation (
4.2.5
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The right vertical and lower horizontal arrows of (
4.2.7
) are isomorphisms by Theorem
4.2.5
.
The upper horizontal arrow of (
4.2.7
) is an isomorphism by Proposition
4.2.7
.
Hence the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism.
Theorem
4.2.5
finishes the proof.
∎

Construction 4.2.10
.

Consider the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{d}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{d}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}

We have the natural transformation

Ex\colon u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)\to\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}

given by the composition

u^{*}e_{*}a_{d}^{!}(ep_{d})^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}e_{*}^{\prime}u^{\prime*}a_{d}^{!}(ep_{d})^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}e_{*}^{\prime}a_{d}^{\prime!}v^{\prime*}(ep_{d})^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}e_{*}^{\prime}a_{d}^{\prime!}(e^{\prime}p_{d}^{\prime})^{*}u^{*}.

Proposition 4.2.11
.

The squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{d}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{n}}

commute.

Proof.

Let us use the notation in Construction
4.2.10
.
Apply
(-)p_{d}^{*}e^{*}
to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}e_{*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}e_{*}i_{1*}i_{1}^{*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}e_{*}i_{1*}a^{!}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}u^{\prime*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}u^{\prime*}i_{1*}i_{1}^{*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}u^{\prime*}i_{1*}a^{!}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}i_{1*}^{\prime}u^{*}i_{1}^{*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}i_{1*}^{\prime}u^{*}a^{!}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}i_{1*}^{\prime}i_{1}^{\prime*}u^{\prime*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}i_{1*}^{\prime}a^{\prime!}v^{*}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}a_{d}^{\prime!}v^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}i_{1*}^{\prime}i_{1}^{\prime*}a_{d}^{\prime!}v^{\prime*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}^{\prime}i_{1*}^{\prime}a^{\prime!}l_{1}^{\prime*}v^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

to see that the left square commutes.
The right square commutes similarly.
∎

Proposition 4.2.12
.

The square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)u_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}

commutes.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}\mathrm{Th}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

All the arrows with the tag
ad
(resp.
Ex
) are isomorphisms by Theorem
4.2.5
(resp. Proposition
4.2.7
).
The upper and lower floors commute by Proposition
4.2.6
.
The right side square commutes by Proposition
4.2.11
.
The left and back side squares commute too, so the front side square commutes.

Together with the fact that
\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})
and
\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})
are equivalences by Theorem
4.2.5
, we deduce that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})u^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

commutes.
We conclude by adjunction.
∎

Proposition 4.2.13
.

The square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(p,a)u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}

commutes.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(p,a)u_{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p,a)u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)u_{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p,a)u_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}\mathrm{Th}_{n}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{*}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm id}}

All the arrows with the tag
ad^{\prime}
(resp.
Ex
) are isomorphisms by Theorem
4.2.5
(resp. Proposition
4.2.9
).
The upper and lower side squares commute by Proposition
4.2.8
.
The back side square commutes by Proposition
4.2.12
.
The front and right side squares commute too, so the left side square commutes.

Together with the fact that
\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})
and
\mathrm{Th}_{n}^{\prime}(p^{\prime},a^{\prime})
are equivalences by Theorem
4.2.5
,
we finish the proof.
∎

such that the inner square is cartesian,
f,p,q,r\in\mathbf{lSmSpc}
,
pa={\rm id}
,
qb={\rm id}
, and
rc={\rm id}
.
We assume that
a
,
b
, and
c
are closed immersions.
We have the natural transformation

C\colon\mathrm{Th}(q,b)\to\mathrm{Th}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}(r,c)

given by the composition

q_{\sharp}b_{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}p_{\sharp}f_{\sharp}g_{*}c_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}p_{\sharp}a_{*}r_{\sharp}c_{*}.

Apply
[
18
, Proposition 9.17]
to
\mathcal{S}\xrightarrow{c}\mathcal{Z}\xrightarrow{g}\mathcal{Y}
to obtain a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{Y}.}

By
[
18
, Lemma 9.7]
, we have an isomorphism
\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{Z}}\mathcal{Y}\simeq\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{Y}
.

is cartesian.
There are induced natural transformations of the diagrams

\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}_{d}\leftarrow\mathcal{Q}_{n}.

We have the natural transformation

C\colon\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(r,c)\to\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(q,b)

given by the composition

e_{*}c_{d}^{!}r_{d}^{*}e_{d}^{*}e_{d*}a_{d}^{!}p_{d}^{*}e^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}e_{*}c_{d}^{!}r_{d}^{*}a_{d}^{!}p_{d}^{*}e^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}e_{*}c_{d}^{!}g_{d}^{!}f_{d}^{*}p_{d}^{*}e^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}e_{*}b_{d}^{!}q_{d}^{*}e^{*}.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

The squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(r,c)\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(p,a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}_{d}^{\prime}(q,b)}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

commute.

Proof.

We only show that the left square commutes since the proofs are similar.
Let
i_{1}\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{S}\times\square
be the
1
-section, let
l_{1}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{X}
,
m_{1}\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{Y}

n_{1}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{Z}
be the pullbacks of
i_{1}
, and let
e\colon S\times\square\to S
be the projection.
Consider the commutative diagram

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ExEx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ExEx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(b)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Hence it suffices to show that the diagrams surrounding
(b)
and
(c)
commute.

Apply
i_{1*}(-)l_{1}^{*}
to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}r_{d}^{*}i_{1*}a^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}r_{d}^{*}a_{d}^{!}l_{1*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}g_{d}^{!}f_{d}^{*}l_{1*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}m_{1}^{*}f_{d}^{*}l_{1*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}n_{1*}r^{*}a^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}n_{1*}g^{!}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}g_{d}^{!}m_{1*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}f^{*}l_{1}^{*}l_{1*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}r^{*}a^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}m_{1}^{*}m_{1*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

to show that the diagram surrounding
(b)
commutes.
Apply
i_{1*}(-)
to the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{*}c_{d}^{!}r_{d}^{*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{*}c_{d}^{!}g_{d}^{!}f_{d}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}^{*}c_{d}^{!}r_{d}^{*}a_{d}^{!}l_{1*}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}r_{d}^{*}a_{d}^{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}m_{1}^{*}f_{d}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}n_{1}^{*}r_{d}^{*}a_{d}^{!}l_{1*}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}f^{*}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}m_{1}^{*}f_{d}^{*}l_{1*}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}f^{*}l_{1}^{*}l_{1*}l_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{!}g^{!}f^{*}l_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ExEx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ExEx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ExEx}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm id}}

to show that the diagram surrounding
(c)
commutes.
∎

5.
Purity

Recall that we fix a sheaf

\mathscr{T}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSch}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))\simeq\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{dNis}(\mathbf{lSpc}/B,\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

satisfying the conditions in the introduction.

In §
5.1
, we define purity transformations and prove their basic properties.
In §
5.2
, we prove that a certain compactification of the morphism
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}^{2}}\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
induced by the diagonal homomorphism
\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}^{2}
is pure.

5.1.
Purity transformations

Definition 5.1.1
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
a
is the diagonal morphism, and
p_{1}
(resp.
p_{2}
) is the first (resp. second) projection.
We set

\Sigma_{f}:=p_{2\sharp}a_{*}\text{ and }\Omega_{f}:=a^{!}p_{2}^{*}.

If we assume also that
f
is proper, then the
purity transformation associated with
f
is defined to be the natural transformation

(5.1.1)

\mathfrak{p}_{f}\colon f_{\sharp}\to f_{*}\Sigma_{f}

given by the composition

f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{\sharp}p_{1*}a_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}f_{*}p_{2\sharp}a_{*}=f_{*}\Sigma_{f}.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
j\colon U\to X
be an open immersion in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

(5.1.2)

\Sigma_{f}j_{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}j_{*}\Sigma_{fj}.

Proof.

Consider the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times_{S}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where
a
,
b
, and
c
are the graph morphisms, and
p_{2}
,
q_{2}
, and
r_{2}
are the second projections.
We have the natural transformations

\Sigma_{f}j_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}j_{*}\mathrm{Th}(q_{2},b)=j_{*}q_{2\sharp}b_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex^{-1}}j_{*}q_{2\sharp}u_{\sharp}c_{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}j_{*}r_{2\sharp}c_{*}=j_{*}\Sigma_{fj}.

The first arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition
4.2.9
.
The third arrow is defined and an isomorphism by Proposition
2.3.5
.
The composition gives the desired natural isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.1.3
.

Suppose that
f\colon X\to S
is a log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
with a section
i
.
We have the natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Sigma_{f}i_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}i_{*}\mathrm{Th}(f,i),\;\mathrm{Th}(f,i)f_{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}f_{*}\Sigma_{f},

\displaystyle i^{*}\Sigma_{f}\xrightarrow{Ex}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(f,i)i^{*},\;f^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(f,i)\xrightarrow{Ex}\Omega_{f}f^{*}.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
a
is the diagonal embedding, and
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are the first and second projections.
Use Propositions
4.2.7
and
4.2.9
for the upper and lower parts of this diagram to conclude.
∎

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
Let
f\colon\mathbb{A}_{S}^{1}\to S
be the projection, and let
i\colon S\to\mathbb{A}_{S}^{1}
be the zero section.
Then the natural transformations

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{*}i^{*}f^{*}\text{ and }f_{\sharp}i_{*}i^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

Consider the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(f,i)f_{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(f,i)f_{*}i_{*}i^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{*}i^{*}f^{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

where the vertical arrows are isomorphisms by Lemma
5.1.3
.
The upper horizontal arrow is an isomorphism by
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance.
It follows that the lower horizontal arrow is an isomorphism.

Consider the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{*}i^{!}f^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(f,i)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}f^{*}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(f,i)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{*}i^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

where the vertical arrows are isomorphisms by Lemma
5.1.3
.
Since
f_{\sharp}i_{*}=\mathrm{Th}(f,i)
, we have an isomorphism
f_{\sharp}i_{*}i^{!}f^{*}\simeq{\rm id}
.
Hence the upper horizontal arrow is an isomorphism by
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance.
It follows that the lower horizontal arrow is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.1.5
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a proper log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
If the two natural transformations

f_{\sharp}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{p}_{f}f^{*}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}\text{ and }f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{p}_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

are isomorphisms, then the natural transformation

\mathfrak{p}_{f}\colon f_{\sharp}\to f_{*}\Sigma_{f}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\alpha
be the composition

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{(\mathfrak{p}_{f}f^{*})^{-1}}f_{\sharp}f^{*}f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}f^{*}f_{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}f_{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}f^{*}f_{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}f^{*}f_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{p}_{f}f^{*}f_{\sharp})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

commutes, which shows that
\alpha
is a left quasi-inverse of
\mathfrak{p}_{f}
.
Let
\beta
be the composition

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\xrightarrow{(\mathfrak{p}_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f})^{-1}}f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp},

where we use the adjunction pair
(\Omega_{f}f^{*},f_{*}\Sigma_{f})
for the first and third natural transformations.
The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{p}_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

commutes, which shows that
\beta
is a right quasi-inverse of
\mathfrak{p}_{f}
.
Hence
\mathfrak{p}_{f}
has both left quasi-inverse and right quasi-inverse, so
\mathfrak{p}_{f}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.1.6
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a proper log smooth morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
, and let
i
be its section.
Then the natural transformation

f_{\sharp}i_{*}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{p}_{f}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the induced commutative diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

The commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}p_{1*}a_{*}i_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}p_{2\sharp}a_{*}i_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}p_{1*}g_{*}i_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}p_{2\sharp}g_{*}i_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}i_{*}f_{\sharp}i_{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

commutes.
The lower right vertical arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition
2.3.5
.
It follows that the composition
f_{\sharp}i_{*}\to f_{*}p_{2\sharp}a_{*}i_{*}
is an isomorphism.
∎

5.2.
Purity for a compactification of
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}^{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}

Throughout this subsection, we fix the following notation.
We set

\displaystyle V_{1}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}x\oplus\mathbb{N}y\to\mathbb{Z}[x,y]),

\displaystyle V_{2}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}(xy)\to\mathbb{Z}[xy,y^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{3}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}(xy)\to\mathbb{Z}[xy,x^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{4}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}(xy)\oplus\mathbb{N}(x^{-1})\to\mathbb{Z}[xy,x^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{5}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}x\oplus\mathbb{N}(xy)\to\mathbb{Z}[x,xy]),

\displaystyle V_{12}:=V_{14}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}x\to\mathbb{Z}[x,y,y^{-1}]),

\displaystyle V_{13}:=V_{35}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}y\to\mathbb{Z}[x,x^{-1},y]),

\displaystyle V_{23}:=V_{34}:=\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[x,x^{-1},y,y^{-1}]).

Let
W
(resp.
W_{5}
,
W_{6}
) be the gluing of
V_{1}
,
V_{2}
, and
V_{3}
(resp.
V_{1}
,
V_{3}
, and
V_{4}
, resp.
V_{3}
and
V_{5}
) along
V_{12}
,
V_{13}
, and
V_{23}
(resp.
V_{13}
,
V_{14}
, and
V_{34}
, resp.
V_{35}
).
Let
W_{2}
(resp.
W_{4}
) be the gluing of
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
(resp.
V_{1}
and
V_{3}
) along
V_{12}
(resp.
V_{13}
). We set
W_{1}:=V_{2}
and
W_{3}:=V_{3}
.
These are fs log schemes over
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}:=\Spec(\mathbb{N}t\to\mathbb{Z}[t])
, where the morphisms
W\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
and
W_{i}\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
for
i=1,\ldots,6
are obtained by the formula
t\mapsto xy
.
We also have

W-W_{2}\simeq V_{3}-V_{13}\simeq\Spec(\mathbb{N}(xy)\to\mathbb{Z}[xy,x^{-1}]/(x^{-1})),

where the closed complements are defined with the reduced scheme structures.
Observe that the induced morphism
W-W_{2}\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
is an isomorphism.

Let
S\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N},B}:=B\times\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
be a morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}/B
.
We set
X:=S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}W
and
X_{i}:=S\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}W_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,6
.
If
u
and
v
are integers, let

j_{uv}\colon X_{u}\to X_{v},\;j_{u}\colon X_{u}\to X,\;i_{uv}\colon X-X_{u}\to X_{v},\text{ and }i_{u}\colon X-X_{u}\to X

be the induced morphisms whenever they are meaningful.
Let
f\colon X\to S
and
f_{i}\colon X_{i}\to S
for
i=1,\ldots,6
be the projections.
We have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X-X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{4}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{5}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{6}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{24}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{25}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{26}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{34}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{4}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{45}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{5}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{56}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{6}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Let us explain the properties of the fs log schemes and morphisms between them constructed above:

•

i_{23}
,
i_{24}
,
i_{25}
,
i_{26}
, and
i_{2}
are closed immersions.

•

j_{34}
,
j_{45}
,
j_{3}
, and
j_{4}
are open immersions.

•

j_{56}
is a dividing cover.

•

The underlying morphism of schemes
\underline{j_{5}}
is an isomorphism.

•

X_{3}\simeq S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
X_{6}\simeq S\times\square
.

•

f
is proper.

•

The composition
X-X_{2}\to S
is an isomorphism.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

The natural transformations

f_{5}^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}j_{45*}j_{45^{*}}f_{5}^{*}\text{ and }f_{6}^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}j_{36*}j_{36^{*}}f_{6}^{*}

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

There are isomorphisms
X_{5}-\partial_{S}X_{5}\simeq X_{4}
and
X_{6}-\partial_{S}X_{6}\simeq X_{3}
.
Proposition
2.3.6
finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.2.2
.

The natural transformation

{\rm id}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{4*}f_{4}^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have natural transformations

f_{5*}f_{5}^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{5*}j_{45*}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{4*}f_{4}^{*},

and the first arrow is an isomorphism by Lemma
5.2.1
.
We similarly have an isomorphism

f_{6*}f_{6}^{*}\simeq f_{3*}f_{3}^{*}.

Since
h_{j_{56}}
is an isomorphism, it suffices to show that the natural transformation

{\rm id}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{3*}f_{3}^{*}

is an isomorphism.
This follows from
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance.
∎

Lemma 5.2.3
.

The natural transformation

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram

(5.2.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}j_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}j_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}f_{4}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}f_{4}^{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

where the upper vertical arrows are obtained by Lemma
5.1.2
.
We have a commutative diagram

(5.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}f_{4}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}f_{4}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5*}j_{45*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5*}j_{45*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5*}\Sigma_{f_{5}}j_{45*}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5*}\Sigma_{f_{5}}j_{45*}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5*}\Sigma_{f_{5}}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5*}\Sigma_{f_{5}}i_{25*}i_{25}^{*}f_{5}^{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

where the middle vertical arrows are obtained by Lemma
5.1.2
, and the left lower vertical arrow is defined and an isomorphism by Lemma
5.2.1
.

We similarly have a commutative diagram

(5.2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3*}\Sigma_{f_{3}}f_{3}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3*}\Sigma_{f_{3}}i_{23*}i_{23}^{*}f_{3}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{6*}\Sigma_{f_{6}}f_{6}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{6*}\Sigma_{f_{6}}i_{26*}i_{26}^{*}f_{6}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

Since
h_{j_{56}}
is an isomorphism, the bottom row of (
5.2.2
) is an isomorphism if and only if the bottom row of (
5.2.3
) is an isomorphism.
Hence it suffices to show that the top row of (
5.2.3
) is an isomorphism.
This follows from Lemma
5.1.4
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.4
.

The natural transformation

f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram

(5.2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}\Omega_{f_{4}}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}\Omega_{f_{4}}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}\Omega_{f_{4}}f_{4}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4\sharp}\Omega_{f_{4}}f_{4}^{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

where the upper vertical arrows are obtained by a left adjoint of (
5.1.2
).
We have a commutative diagram

(5.2.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}\Omega_{f_{4}}f_{4}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{4\sharp}\Omega_{f_{4}}f_{4}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5\sharp}j_{45\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}\Omega_{f_{4}}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5\sharp}j_{45\sharp}\Omega_{f_{4}}j_{45}^{*}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5\sharp}j_{45\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}j_{45}^{*}\Omega_{f_{5}}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5\sharp}j_{45\sharp}j_{45}^{*}\Omega_{f_{5}}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5\sharp}i_{25*}i_{25}^{!}\Omega_{f_{5}}f_{5}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{5\sharp}\Omega_{f_{5}}f_{5}^{*},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

where the middle vertical arrows are obtained by the left adjoint of (
5.1.2
), the left below vertical arrow is an isomorphism by
Proposition
2.3.4
, and the right below vertical arrow is defined and an isomorphism by Lemma
5.2.1
.

We similarly have a commutative diagram

(5.2.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3\sharp}i_{23*}i_{23}^{!}\Omega_{f_{3}}f_{3}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{3\sharp}\Omega_{f_{3}}f_{3}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{6\sharp}i_{26*}i_{26}^{!}\Omega_{f_{6}}f_{6}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{6\sharp}\Omega_{f_{6}}f_{6}^{*}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}

Since
h_{j_{56}}
is an isomorphism, the bottom row of (
5.2.5
) is an isomorphism if and only if the bottom row of (
5.2.6
) is an isomorphism.
Hence it suffices to show that the top row of (
5.2.6
) is an isomorphism.
This follows from Lemma
5.1.4
.
∎

Observe that
fi_{4}\colon X-X_{4}\to S
is an isomorphism.

Lemma 5.2.5
.

The sequence

f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.

Proof.

The localization property implies that the sequence

f_{\sharp}j_{2\sharp}j_{2}^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.
Hence it suffices to show that the composition

f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{4\sharp}f_{4}^{*}

is an isomorphism, where the first arrow is defined and an isomorphism by Proposition
2.3.4
.

Since
f_{\sharp}i_{4*}=\mathrm{Th}(f,i_{4})
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories by Theorem
4.2.5
, we have a natural isomorphism
f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}\simeq{\rm id}
.
Lemma
5.2.2
finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.2.6
.

The sequence

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.

Proof.

The localization property implies that the sequence

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.
We conclude together with Lemma
5.2.3
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.7
.

The sequence

f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.

Proof.

After switching the coordinates
x
and
y
in the formulations of
V_{1}
,
V_{2}
, and
V_{3}
,
we can instead show that the sequence

f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.

The localization property implies that the sequence

f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.
Hence it suffices to show that the composition

f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}i_{24*}i_{24}^{!}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{\sharp}j_{4\sharp}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is an isomorphism, where the first arrow is defined and an isomorphism by Proposition
2.3.4
.
This follows from Lemma
5.2.4
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.8
.

The sequence

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.

Proof.

The localization sequence implies that the sequence

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is a cofiber sequence.
Hence it suffices to show that the composition

f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Lemma
5.1.2
gives a natural isomorphism
\Sigma_{f}j_{4*}\simeq j_{4*}\Sigma_{f_{4}}
, whose left adjoint is
\Omega_{f_{4}}j_{4}^{*}\simeq j_{4}^{*}\Omega_{f}
.
Lemma
5.1.3
gives natural isomorphisms
\Sigma_{f}i_{2*}\simeq i_{2*}\mathrm{Th}(f,i_{2})
and
i_{2}^{*}\Omega_{f}\simeq\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(f,i_{2})i_{2}^{*}
.
Hence it suffices to show that the composition

f_{*}j_{4*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}j_{4*}i_{24*}i_{24}^{*}j_{4}^{*}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{*}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}f^{*}

is an isomorphism.
This follows from Lemma
5.2.2
since
fi_{2}\colon X-X_{2}\to S
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.2.9
.

The natural transformation

f_{\sharp}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{p}_{f}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

whose rows are cofiber sequences by Lemmas
5.2.5
and
5.2.6
.
The left and right vertical arrows are isomorphisms by Lemma
5.1.6
.
It follows that the middle vertical arrow is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.2.10
.

The natural transformation

f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{p}_{f}}f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{4*}i_{4}^{!}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\Sigma_{f}i_{2*}i_{2}^{*}\Omega_{f}f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}

whose rows are cofiber sequences by Lemmas
5.2.7
and
5.2.8
.
The left and right vertical arrows are isomorphisms by Lemma
5.1.6
.
It follows that the middle vertical arrow is an isomorphism.
∎

Theorem 5.2.11
.

The natural transformation

\mathfrak{p}_{f}\colon f_{\sharp}\to f_{*}\Sigma_{f}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Combine Lemmas
5.1.5
,
5.2.9
, and
5.2.10
.
∎

The following result is needed in
[
19
]
.
We are still using the notation
f\colon X\to S
constructed at the beginning of this subsection.

Proposition 5.2.12
.

Let
d\colon S^{\prime}\to S
be a strict closed immersion with its open complement
u\colon S^{\prime\prime}\to S
.
Consider the induced commutative diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

Then we have
d^{*}f_{*}u_{\sharp}^{\prime}\simeq 0
.

Proof.

Let
a^{\prime\prime}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X\times_{S}X^{\prime\prime}
be the graph morphism, and let
p^{\prime\prime}\colon X\times_{S}X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime\prime}
be the projection.
A left adjoint of (
4.2.4
) gives an isomorphism

(5.2.7)

\Omega_{f}u_{\sharp}^{\prime}\simeq u_{\sharp}^{\prime}\mathrm{Th}^{\prime}(p^{\prime\prime},a^{\prime\prime}).

Theorem
5.2.11
gives an isomorphism

(5.2.8)

d^{*}f_{*}u_{\sharp}^{\prime}\simeq d^{*}f_{\sharp}\Omega_{f}u_{\sharp}^{\prime}.

Combine (
5.2.7
) and (
5.2.8
), and use
d^{*}f_{\sharp}u_{\sharp}^{\prime}\simeq d^{*}u_{\sharp}f_{\sharp}^{\prime\prime}\simeq 0
to conclude.
∎

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

be a commutative square in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
.
Assume that
f^{*}
and
f^{\prime*}
admit left adjoints
f_{\sharp}
and
f_{\sharp}^{\prime}
.
The
Beck-Chevalley transformation
(or
exchange transformation
) is defined to be the composition

Ex\colon f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}f^{*}f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}f^{\prime*}g^{*}f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}g^{*}f_{\sharp}.

Definition A.1.2
.

Let
Q
be a commutative square in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
of the form (
A.1.1
).
We say that
Q
admits a left adjoint
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(i)

f^{*}
and
f^{\prime*}
admit left adjoints
f_{\sharp}
and
f_{\sharp}^{\prime}
.

(ii)

The exchange transformation
Ex\colon f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\to g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is an isomorphism.

In this case, we say that the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}}

is a
left adjoint of
Q
.

Remark A.1.3
.

We are following Liu-Zheng
[
9
, Definition 1.4.1]
for the above terminology of a left adjoint of a square.
Lurie’s terminology
[
10
, Definition 7.3.1.2]
is that
Q
is
left adjointable
.

Example A.1.4
.

Let
Q
be a commutative square in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
of the form (
A.1.1
).
Assume that
f^{*}
and
f^{\prime*}
admit left adjoints
f_{\sharp}
and
f_{\sharp}^{\prime}
and
g^{*}
and
g^{\prime*}
admit right adjoints
g_{*}
and
g_{*}^{\prime}
.
If the exchange transformation
Ex\colon f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}\to g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is an isomorphism, then we have another exchange transformation

Ex\colon f_{\sharp}g_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{ad}g_{*}g^{*}f_{\sharp}g_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{Ex^{-1}}g_{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}g^{\prime*}g_{*}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}g_{*}f_{\sharp}^{\prime}.

A.2.
Extensions of functors to the category of correspondences

The purpose of this section is to review the technique of partial adjoints developed by Liu-Zheng
[
9
]
.

We first recall the definition of the
\infty
-category of correspondences in
[
9
, §6.1]
as follows.
For every integer
n\geq 0
, let
\mathbf{C}(\Delta^{n})
be the full subcategory of
\Delta^{n}\times(\Delta^{n})^{op}
spanned by
(i,j)
such that
i\leq j
.
This construction defines a colimit preserving functor
\mathbf{C}\colon\mathbf{Set}_{\Delta}\to\mathbf{Set}_{\Delta}
.
Let
\mathrm{Corr}\colon\mathbf{Set}_{\Delta}\to\mathbf{Set}_{\Delta}
be its right adjoint.

Observe that
\mathbf{C}(\Delta^{n})
is the nerve of the ordinary category associated with the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,n-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(0,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1,n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1,1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(n-1,n-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(n-1,n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(n-1,n-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(n,n).}

A morphism of
\mathbf{C}(\Delta^{n})
is
vertical
(resp.
horizontal
) if its image in the second (resp. first) factor degenerates.
A square in
\mathbf{C}(\Delta^{n})
is
exact
if it is cartesian and cocartesian, i.e., it is a square of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m_{1},n_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m_{1},n_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m_{2},n_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m_{2},n_{2})}

for some integers
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
,
n_{1}
, and
n_{2}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is an
\infty
-category and
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are class of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
.
The
simplicial set of correspondences

\mathrm{Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is the simplicial subset of
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathcal{C})
whose
n
-cells
\mathbf{C}(\Delta^{n})\to\mathcal{C}
sends vertical (resp. horizontal) morphisms into
\mathcal{P}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}
) and exact squares to pullback squares.
This is an
\infty
-category if
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are closed under pullbacks along one another and compositions by
[
9
, Lemma 6.1.2]
.

A morphism in
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
can be expressed as a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(0,1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(0,0)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(1,1)}}

in
\mathcal{C}
such that the vertical morphism is in
\mathcal{P}
and the horizontal morphism is in
\mathcal{Q}
.
A composition of morphisms can be expressed as a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(0,2)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(0,1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(0,0)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(1,2)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(1,1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{(2,2)}}

in
\mathcal{C}
such that the vertical morphisms are in
\mathcal{P}
, the horizontal morphisms are in
\mathcal{Q}
, and the inner square is cartesian.
To simplify notation, we set

Theorem A.2.1

(Liu-Zheng)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a class of morphisms in an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
closed under compositions and pullbacks, and let
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathcal{C}^{op}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
be a functor.
Assume that
\mathcal{F}(f)
admits a left adjoint for every
f\in\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{F}(Q)
admits a left adjoint for every cartesian square

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{30.10678pt}{-2.90672pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.02588pt}{-14.16367pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
