\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

in
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all})
.
Then
\mathcal{F}(Q^{\dagger})
is a left adjoint of
\mathcal{F}(Q)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all}}^{\mathrm{cart}}
be the cartesian bisimplicial nerve of
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all})
in the sense of
[
9
, Definition 1.3.6]
.
The
(m,n)
-simplicies of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all}}^{\mathrm{cart}}
are the maps

\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{n}\to\mathcal{C}

sending
e\times v
to a morphism in
\mathcal{P}
and
e\times f
to a cartesian square for every vertex
v
in
\Delta^{n}
, edge
e
in
\Delta^{m}
, and edge
f
in
\Delta^{n}
.
We employ the functor

\delta_{2,\{2\}}\colon\mathbf{Set}_{\Delta\times\Delta}\to\mathbf{Set}_{\Delta}

in
[
9
, Definition 1.3.2(4)]
.

By
[
8
, Example 4.30]
(see also
[
9
, §6.1]
),
there is a categorical equivalence

\delta_{2,\{2\}}^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all}}^{\mathrm{cart}}\to\mathrm{Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all}).

On the other hand,
[
9
, Proposition 1.4.4]
provides a functor

\delta_{2,\{2\}}^{*}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all}}^{\mathrm{cart}}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}.

Combine these two to obtain

\mathcal{F}\colon\mathrm{Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P},\mathrm{all})\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
.
The conclusions (1) and (4) in loc. cit. imply that this is a natural extension of the original
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathcal{C}^{op}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
.
The conclusions (2) and (3) in loc. cit. imply our conditions (i) and (ii).
∎

A.3.
Equivalences of
\infty
-topoi

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an ordinary category.
Consider the functor
\iota\colon\mathbf{Set}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
sending a set to the corresponding discrete space.
This induces a functor

\iota\colon\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}).

For
X\in\mathcal{C}
, let

p_{X}\in\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})\text{ and }\mathfrak{p}_{X}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})

be the presheaves represented by
X
.
These two are related by

(A.3.1)

\mathfrak{p}_{X}\simeq\iota p_{X}.

A presheaf of spaces
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
is a
sheaf
if
\mathcal{F}
satisfies Čech descent,
i.e.,
we have

\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\lim\big(\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}(X_{i})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\prod_{i,j\in I}\mathcal{F}(X_{i}\times_{X}X_{j})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\cdots\big)

for every covering
\{X_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
.
This definition of sheaves of spaces is equivalent to that in
[
10
, Definition 6.2.2.6]
by
[
7
, Theorem A5]
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
consisting of sheaves,
which is the localization of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
with respect to the class of morphisms

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\big(\cdots\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i,j\in I}\mathfrak{p}_{X_{i}\times_{X}X_{j}}\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i\in I}\mathfrak{p}_{X_{i}}\big)\to\mathfrak{p}_{X}

for every covering
\{X_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a site.
We have an adjunction

a^{*}:\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C}):a_{*},

where
a^{*}
is the sheafification functor,
and
a_{*}
is the inclusion functor.
We have an adjunction

L^{*}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}):L_{*},

where
L^{*}
is the localization functor,
and
L_{*}
is the inclusion functor.
For
X\in\mathcal{C}
,
let

h_{X}\in\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})\text{ and }\mathfrak{h}_{X}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})

be the sheaves represented by
X
.
There are relations

h_{X}\simeq a^{*}p_{X}\text{ and }\mathfrak{h}_{X}\simeq L^{*}\mathfrak{p}_{X}.

Let

\iota\colon\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})

be the composition
L^{*}\iota a_{*}
.

Proposition A.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a site.
Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

commutes.
In particular,

\iota h_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}_{X}
for all
X\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The natural transformation

L^{*}\iota\xrightarrow{ad}L^{*}\iota a_{*}a^{*}

is an isomorphism by
[
7
, Proposition A2]
,
which shows the claim.
∎

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a functor of categories.
By
[
1
, Proposition I.5.1]
,
there is an adjunction

f^{*}:\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{D}):f_{*}

such that
f_{*}\mathcal{G}(X):=\mathcal{G}(f(X))
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{D})
and
X\in\mathcal{C}
.
By
[
10
, Proposition 5.2.6.3]
,
there is an adjunction

f^{*}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}):f_{*}

such that
f_{*}\mathcal{G}(X):=\mathcal{G}(f(X))
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
and
X\in\mathcal{C}
.
Since this
f_{*}
preserves colimits, there is an adjunction

f_{*}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}):f^{!}.

If
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a continuous functor of sites,
then by
[
1
, Proposition III.1.2]
,
there is an adjunction

f^{*}:\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})\rightleftarrows\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D}):f_{*}

such that
f_{*}\mathcal{G}(X):=\mathcal{G}(f(X))
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})
and
X\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proposition A.3.2
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a functor of categories.
Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

commutes.

Proof.

Observe that we have natural isomorphisms

\iota f_{*}\mathcal{G}(Y)\simeq\mathcal{G}(f(Y))\simeq f_{*}\iota\mathcal{G}(Y)

for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{D})
and
Y\in\mathcal{D}
.
∎

Definition A.3.3
.

We say that a functor of sites
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

preserves Čech coverings
if the following conditions are satisfied for every covering
\{X_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{C}
:

(i)

\{f(X_{i})\to f(X)\}_{i\in I}
is a covering in
\mathcal{D}
.

(ii)

f(X_{i_{1}})\times_{f(X)}\cdots\times_{f(X)}f(X_{i_{n}})
is representable and the induced morphism

f(X_{i_{1}}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}X_{i_{n}})\to f(X_{i_{1}})\times_{f(X)}\cdots\times_{f(X)}f(X_{i_{n}})

is an isomorphism for all
i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\in I
.

Proposition A.3.4
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a functor of sites preserving Čech coverings.
Then there is an adjunction

(A.3.2)

f^{*}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}):f_{*},

where
f_{*}\mathcal{G}(X):=\mathcal{G}(f(X))
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
and
X\in\mathcal{C}
.
Furthermore,
the square

(A.3.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}}

commutes.

Proof.

Let us show that
f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G}
is a sheaf for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
.
We have

f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G}(X)\simeq\mathcal{G}(f(X))

for all
X\in\mathcal{C}
.
Let
\{X_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
be a covering in
\mathcal{C}
.
Then
\{f(X_{i})\to f(X)\}_{i\in I}
is a covering in
\mathcal{D}
since
f
preserves Čech covering.
Furthermore,

\mathcal{G}(f(X))\simeq\lim\big(\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}(f(X_{i}))\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\prod_{i,j\in I}\mathcal{G}(f(X_{i})\times_{f(X)}f(X_{j}))\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\cdots\big)

implies

f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(X)\simeq\lim\big(\prod_{i\in I}f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(X_{i})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\prod_{i,j\in I}f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(X_{i}\times_{X}X_{j})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\cdots\big).

Hence
f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G}
is a sheaf.
It follows that the natural transformation

(A.3.4)

f_{*}L_{*}\xrightarrow{ad}L_{*}L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}

is an isomorphism.

For all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
, consider the morphisms

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(L^{*}f^{*}L_{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\simeq}

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(L_{*}\mathcal{F},f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(L^{*}L_{*}\mathcal{F},L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(\mathcal{F},L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G}).

The second arrow is an isomorphism since (
A.3.4
) is an isomorphism.
It follows that
L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}
is a right adjoint of
L^{*}f^{*}L_{*}
.
Since

L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G}(X)\simeq f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G}(X)\simeq\mathcal{G}(f(X)),

we have

(A.3.5)

f_{*}\simeq L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}.

We set
f^{*}:=L^{*}f^{*}L_{*}
to obtain (
A.3.2
).
From (
A.3.4
),
we have
f_{*}L_{*}\simeq L_{*}f_{*}
.
Take left adjoints to see that (
A.3.3
) commutes.
∎

Proposition A.3.5
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a continuous and cocontinuous functor of sites preserving Čech coverings.
Assume that

f_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})\to\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})

is an equivalence of categories.
Then
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
preserves colimits,
and
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Recall from (
A.3.5
) that we have
f_{*}\simeq L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}
.
Let us first show that
L^{*}f^{!}L_{*}
is a right adjoint of
L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}
.
For this,
it suffices to show that
f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf for all
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
since this implies that the second arrow in

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(L^{*}f_{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(L_{*}\mathcal{G},f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(L^{*}L_{*}\mathcal{G},L^{*}f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(\mathcal{G},L^{*}f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F})

is an isomorphism for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
.
We have isomorphisms

L^{*}f_{*}\mathfrak{p}_{Y}\simeq L^{*}f_{*}\iota p_{Y}\simeq L^{*}\iota f_{*}p_{Y}\simeq\iota a^{*}f_{*}p_{Y}\simeq\iota f_{*}a^{*}p_{Y}\simeq\iota f_{*}h_{Y}.

for all
Y\in\mathcal{D}
,
where we need Proposition
A.3.2
for the second isomorphism, Proposition
A.3.1
for the third isomorphism, and
[
1
, Proposition III.2.3(3)]
for the fourth isomorphism.
Hence we have a natural isomorphism

f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(Y)\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}(\iota f_{*}h_{Y},\mathcal{F}).

We need to show

f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(Y)\simeq\lim\big(\prod_{i\in I}f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(Y_{i})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\to\\[-10.00002pt]
\to\end{subarray}\;\prod_{i,j\in I}f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F}(Y_{i}\times_{Y}Y_{j})\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\cdots\big)

for every covering
\{Y_{i}\to Y\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{D}
.
To show this,
it suffices to show

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\big(\cdots\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i,j\in I}\iota f_{*}h_{Y_{i}\times_{Y}Y_{j}}\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i\in I}\iota f_{*}h_{Y_{i}}\big)\simeq\iota f_{*}h_{Y}.

By
[
7
, Corollary A3]
,
it suffices to show that

\cdots\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i,j\in I}f_{*}h_{Y_{i}\times_{Y}Y_{j}}\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i\in I}f_{*}h_{Y_{i}}\to f_{*}h_{Y}

is a Čech nerve and
f_{*}h_{Y_{i}}\to f_{*}h_{Y}
is an epimorphism.
This is a consequence of the assumption that

f_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})\to\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})

is an equivalence.
Hence we have shown that
f^{!}L_{*}\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf for all
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
and
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
preserves colimits.

It follows that the natural transformation

f^{!}L_{*}\xrightarrow{ad}L_{*}L^{*}f^{!}L_{*}

is an isomorphism.
Take left adjoints to see that the right square in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Psh}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}}

commutes.
The middle square commutes by Proposition
A.3.2
.
The left square commutes since
f
is a continuous functor of sites.
It follows that the square

(A.3.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

commutes.
From this, we have the exchange transformation

\iota f^{*}\xrightarrow{Ex}f^{*}\iota.

We claim that the morphism

(A.3.7)

\iota f^{*}h_{X}\xrightarrow{Ex}f^{*}\iota h_{X}

is an isomorphism for all
X\in\mathcal{C}
.
Consider the induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota a^{*}f^{*}p_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota f^{*}a^{*}p_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\iota a^{*}p_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}\iota f^{*}p_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{*}f^{*}\iota p_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}L^{*}\iota p_{X}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

The left vertical arrow is an isomorphism since (
A.3.6
) commutes.
The lower right horizontal arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition
A.3.4
.
From

\displaystyle\iota a^{*}p_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{h}_{X}\simeq L^{*}\iota p_{X},

\displaystyle a^{*}f^{*}p_{X}\simeq h_{f(X)}\simeq f^{*}a^{*}p_{X},

\displaystyle\iota f^{*}p_{X}\simeq\mathfrak{p}_{f(X)}\simeq f^{*}\iota p_{X},

we see that the right vertical, upper left horizontal, and lower left horizontal arrows are isomorphisms.
It follows that the upper left horizontal arrow is an isomorphism,
i.e., (
A.3.7
) is an isomorphism.

To show that
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
is fully faithful,
it suffices to show that the morphism

(A.3.8)

\mathfrak{h}_{X}\xrightarrow{ad}f_{*}f^{*}\mathfrak{h}_{X}

is an isomorphism for all
X\in\mathcal{C}
since every sheaf of spaces is isomorphic to a colimit of representable sheaves of spaces and
f^{*}
and
f_{*}
preserve colimits.
Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota h_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota f_{*}f^{*}h_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\iota f^{*}h_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}f^{*}\iota h_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ad}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ex}

The right vertical arrow is an isomorphism since (
A.3.7
) is an isomorphism.
The upper left horizontal arrow is an isomorphism since
f_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})\to\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})
is an equivalence.
It follows that the diagonal arrow is an isomorphism,
i.e., (
A.3.8
) is an isomorphism.
∎

Theorem A.3.6
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a continuous and cocontinuous functor of sites preserving Čech coverings.
Assume the following conditions:

(i)

For all
Y\in\mathcal{D}
, there exists a covering
\{Y_{i}\to Y\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{D}
such that

Y_{i_{1}}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{i_{n}}\in\im f

for all
i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\in I
.

(ii)

f_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{D})\to\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{C})

is an equivalence,

Then
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{V})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{V})
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories for every
\infty
-category
\mathcal{V}
admitting limits.

Proof.

By
[
12
, Proposition 1.3.1.7]
,
there is an equivalence between
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{V})
and the
\infty
-category of functors from
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
to
\mathcal{V}
sending colimits to limits for every site
\mathcal{E}
.
Hence we reduce to the case when
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}
.

Proposition
A.3.5
means that
f^{*}
is fully faithful and
f_{*}
preserves colimits.
Hence it suffices to show that the morphism

f^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}\mathcal{G}

is an isomorphism for all
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc})
.
Since
f^{*}
preserves colimits and
\mathcal{G}
is a colimit of representable sheaves of spaces,
it suffices to show that the morphism

f^{*}f_{*}\mathfrak{h}_{Y}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}\mathfrak{h}_{Y}

is an isomorphism for all
Y\in\mathcal{D}
.
Consider the covering
\{Y_{i}\to Y\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{D}
in the assumption.
Since

\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\big(\cdots\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i,j\in I}\mathfrak{h}_{Y_{i}\times_{Y}Y_{j}}\;\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\[-10.00002pt]
\rightarrow\end{subarray}\;\coprod_{i\in I}\mathfrak{h}_{Y_{i}}\big)\simeq\mathfrak{h}_{Y},

we only need to show that the morphism

f^{*}f_{*}\mathfrak{h}_{Y_{i_{1}}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{i_{n}}}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}\mathfrak{h}_{Y_{i_{1}}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{i_{n}}}

is an isomorphism for all
i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\in I
.
There exists
X\in\mathcal{C}
such that
f(X)\simeq Y_{i_{1}}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{i_{n}}
.
This implies
\mathfrak{h}_{Y_{i_{1}}\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}Y_{i_{n}}}\simeq f^{*}\mathfrak{h}_{X}
.
To conclude,
observe that the composition

f^{*}\mathfrak{h}_{X}\xrightarrow{ad}f^{*}f_{*}f^{*}\mathfrak{h}_{X}\xrightarrow{ad^{\prime}}f^{*}\mathfrak{h}_{X}

is the identity and the first arrow is an isomorphism since
f^{*}
is fully faithful.
∎

A.4.
Sheaves and cd-structures

Recall from
[
21
]
that a
cd-structure

P
on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object is a collection of commutative squares.

Definition A.4.1
.

Let
P
be a cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object,
and let
\mathcal{V}
is an
\infty
-category admitting limits.
A presheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{V})
is
excisive
if the square
\mathcal{F}(Q)
is cartesian for all
Q\in P
.

The following result is a variant of
[
21
, Corollary 5.10]
.
See also
[
2
, Theorem 3.2.5]
.

Theorem A.4.2

(Voevodsky)
.

Let
P
be a cd-structure on a category
\mathcal{C}
with an initial object.
We regard
\mathcal{C}
as a site with the topology associated with the cd-structure.
Assume that for every square

(A.4.1)

Q:=\hbox to83.68pt{\vbox to51.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 41.83865pt\lower-24.03725pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

is in
P
,
where the vertical arrows are the diagonal morphisms.

Then a presheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{V})
is a sheaf if and only if it is excisive,
where
\mathcal{V}
is an
\infty
-category admitting limits.

Proof.

Let
Q
be an element of
P
of the form (
A.4.1
).

Assume that
\mathcal{F}
is excisive.
The coverings

\{S^{\prime}\times_{S}S^{\prime},X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}\},\;\{S^{\prime}\times_{S}X,X\times_{S}X\to X\},\text{ and }\{S^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\prime},X\times_{S}X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}\}

have sections,
and any presheaf satisfies Čech descent for a covering with a section.
Hence
\mathcal{F}
satisfies Čech descent for these coverings.
Use the assumption that
\mathcal{F}
is excisive to see that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies Čech descent for the covering
\{S^{\prime},X\to S\}
.
Together with
[
21
, Lemmas 2.4, 2.5]
,
we deduce that
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf.

For the converse,
assume that
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf.
Let
X_{\bullet}\to S
and
X_{\bullet}^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
be the Čech nerves of
X\to S
and
X^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
.
The terms in the Čech nerve of
S^{\prime}\amalg X\to S
are coproducts of objects of the forms
S^{\prime}
,
X_{i}
, and
X_{i}^{\prime}
with
i\geq 0
.
Hence to show that
\mathcal{F}
is excisive for
Q
,
it suffices to show that
\mathcal{F}
is excisive for

Q\times_{S}S^{\prime},\;Q\times_{S}X_{i},\text{ and }Q\times_{S}X_{i}^{\prime}

with
i\geq 0
.
The horizontal arrows in

are isomorphisms,
so
\mathcal{F}
is excisive for
Q\times_{S}S^{\prime}
and
Q\times_{S}X_{i}^{\prime}
.
The square
Q\times_{S}X_{i}
is the bottom square of the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i+1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i},}

where the upper square is
R_{i}:=R\times_{X\times_{S}X}X_{i+1}
and
R
is the square (
A.4.2
).
It suffices to show that
\mathcal{F}
is excisive for
R_{i}
.
The vertical arrows in the square
R_{i}
are monomorphisms.
This means that we only need to show that
\mathcal{F}
is excisive for
Q
when
X\to S
is a monomorphism.
In this case,
the vertical arrows in the square
Q\times_{S}X_{i}
are isomorphisms.
Hence
\mathcal{F}
is excisive.
∎
